# Tractate Semachot

מסכת שמחות

Link: [https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot)

דינים ומנהגים הקשורים למוות: רגע המוות, קבורה, אבלות וכללי התנהגות בבית הקברות.

Laws and customs relating to death: the moment of death, burial, mourning, and cemetery conduct.

Sections: Paragraph

Location: Talmud/Bavli/Minor Tractates

Originally from [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org) via [TorahLM.org](https://torahlm.org) — see [licensing](https://torahlm.org/sefaria_license).

Version (He): Talmud Bavli, Vilna 1883 ed. (Segments: 231, License: Public Domain)

Version (En): The Minor Tractates of the Talmud, trans. A. Cohen, London: Soncino Press, 1965 (Segments: 231)


## Introduction

###### Tractate Semachot, Introduction 1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/s/Introduction/r/1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/s/Introduction/r/1)


(שמות י״ב:כ״ט) ויהי בחצי הלילה וה׳ הכה כל בכור בארץ מצרים וגו׳ וכתיב (במדבר ח׳:י״ז) כי לי כל בכור [וגו׳] ביום הכותי כל בכור וגו' (ואתה משיב) א״ר יוחנן אע״פ שהכה אותם מכת מות מחצי הלילה היתה נפשם מפרפרת בהם עד הבוקר משל הדיוט אומר נתת פת לנער הודע את אמו אף הקדוש ברוך הוא אמר אודיע לבני המיתה המשתקת הזאת ששונאיהם היאך מתים בה אלא תשמור נפשותם עד הבוקר כדי שיראו בני בשונאיהם:


*And it came to pass at midnight, that the Lord smote all the first-born in the land of Egypt*,² etc.; and it is written, *For all the first-born among the children of Israel are Mine, both men and beast; on the day that I smote all the first-born in the land of Egypt, I sanctified them for Myself*.³ R. Joḥanan said: Although He smote them with the plague of death at midnight, the souls writhed within them until morning. A common proverb says, ‘If you give a morsel of bread to a child, inform his mother’.⁴ Likewise, the Holy One, blessed be He, said, ‘I will inform My children of the death by choking⁵ of which their enemies are dying. Let their souls await the morning [before expiring], so that My children shall look upon [the fate of] their enemies’.

footnotes:
² Ex. 12, 29.
³ Num. 8, 17.
⁴ Cf. Beẓah 16a (Sonc. ed., p. 81) where it is quoted, not as a proverb, but as a saying of Rabban Simeon b. Gamaliel.
⁵ GRA’s emendation of a verb in the text meaning ‘which silences’.



## Chapter 1

###### Tractate Semachot 1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:1)


הגוסס הרי הוא כחי לכל דבר לעולם (זקוק) [זוקק] ליבום ופוטר מן היבום ומאכיל בתרומה ופוסל בתרומה ונוחל ומנחיל פרש ממנו אבר כאבר מן החי בשר כבשר מן החי וזורקין על ידו דם חטאתו ודם אשמו עד שימות:


A dying man¹, the term applied when the rattle in the throat indicates that a person is at the point of death. is regarded as a living entity in respect of all matters in the world: he holds [his brother’s wife] tied to the leviratical marriage,² he frees [his mother] from the obligation of a leviratical marriage³ and enables her to eat *terumah*,⁴, she may continue to eat *terumah* so long as he remains at the point of death. and he [may also] disqualify her from eating *terumah;*⁵ married to a lay-Israelite, she may not eat of the *terumah* belonging to her father which, on the death of her husband, she is permitted to eat when she returns to his home (cf. Lev. 22, 12f). The reason is that, since her son is regarded as living, it would be contrary to the stipulation in Lev. *and have no child*. he inherits and transmits;⁶ if one of his limbs is severed, it is regarded as a limb cut off from the living;⁷ if [part of his] flesh [is severed], it is regarded as flesh cut off from the living;⁸ and the blood of his sin-offering and guilt-offering is sprinkled for him until the time he dies.⁹

footnotes:
¹ Heb. gosēs
² Cf. Deut. 25, 5ff. Should the brother die while he himself is dying, the former’s widow is precluded from marrying until she is released by the latter from his obligation to wed her (cf. Ohol. I, 6, Sonc. ed., pp. 151f).
³ If his father died and left no other children, his mother is free to remarry because the dying man is regarded as living. Even a child who survives one day frees his mother from the levirate. Cf. Nid. 43b (Sonc. ed., p. 303).
⁴ If his mother is the daughter of a lay-Israelite married to a kohen
⁵ If she is the daughter of a kohen
⁶ i.e. an estate. On inheritance and transmission, cf. B.B. 108a (Sonc. ed., p. 448).
⁷ There is a legal difference if it is a limb severed from a living or dead body. If it was from a dead body, a piece of flesh from it, even the size of an olive, communicates defilement; but if from a living body it does not. Therefore, if one of the dying man’s limbs is severed, flesh from it does not defile because he is considered a living being.
⁸ Flesh not necessarily from a severed limb.
⁹ Where a man who brought a sin- or guilt-offering died before it was sacrificed, the animal for the former had to be killed and the latter turned into the fields to graze until it died naturally. But with a dying man who brought an offering, the ritual connected with his sacrifice was carried out completely.


###### Tractate Semachot 1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:2)


אין קושרין את לחייו ואין פוקקין את נקביו ואין נותנין כלי של מתכות ודבר שהוא מיקר על טיבורו עד שעה שימות שנאמר (קהלת י״ב:ו׳) עד אשר לא ירתק חבל הכסף:


We do not tie up his cheek-bones,¹⁰ or stop up his apertures,¹¹ or place a metal vessel or anything which chills on his navel;¹² reads ‘on his stomach’, that it should not swell. as it is stated, *Before the silver cord is snapped asunder and the golden bowl is shattered, and the pitcher is broken at the fountain.*¹³

footnotes:
¹⁰ To prevent the jaw from dropping; cf. Shab. 151a (Sonc. ed., p. 771).
¹¹ To prevent air from entering.
¹² Shab. loc. cit.
¹³ Eccl. 12, 6. None of these acts may be done because they hasten death.


###### Tractate Semachot 1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:3)


אין מזיזין אותו ואין מטילין אותו על גבי החול ולא על המלח עד שעה שימות:


We may not move him, or place him on sand or salt¹⁴ until he dies.¹⁵

footnotes:
¹⁴ For ‘salt’ GRA reads ‘a vessel that chills’; cf. Tosiftha Shab. XVIII, 18.
¹⁵ So that the body should keep cool and not swell.


###### Tractate Semachot 1:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:4)


אין (מעמידין) [מעמצין] את עיני הגוסס הנוגע בו ומזיזו הרי זה שופך דמים שהיה רבי מאיר אומר משל לנר שהוא מטפטף כיון שנגע בו אדם מכבהו כך כל המעמץ את עיני הגוסס מעלין עליו כאילו הוא נוטל נשמתו:


We may not close the eyes¹⁶ (Sonc. ed., p. 772). Closing the eyes of a corpse is a very ancient practice. According to Ibn Ezra the custom is alluded to in Gen. 46, 4, *and Joseph shall put his hand upon thine eyes* (cf. his commentary *ad loc*.). The custom was current among the ancient Greeks and Romans and is practised by the Egyptians [cf. Seyffert, *Dictionary of Classical Antiquities*, p. 101b, and Lane, *Manners and Customs of the Modern Egyptians*, ch. XXVIII]. A man, at the moment of death, was believed to behold the Divine Presence (cf. Job 19, 27) and therefore it was unfitting that after that vision his eyes should look at anything mundane. Cf. A. Bender in *Jewish Quarterly Review*, VII (1895), pp. 102f. of a dying man. Whoever touches and moves him is a murderer. For R. Meir used to say: He can be compared to a lamp which is dripping;¹⁷ reads ‘a lamp that is going out’. should a man touch it he extinguishes it. Similarly whoever closes the eyes of a dying man is considered as if he had taken his life.

footnotes:
¹⁶ Shab. loc. cit.
¹⁷ Shab. loc cit.


###### Tractate Semachot 1:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:5)


אין קורעין ואין חולצין ולא מספידין ואין מכניסין ארון עמו בבית עד שעה שימות:


We do not rend the garments or bare the shoulder,¹⁸ in the usual sense of ‘removing the shoes’. or deliver a memorial address, or bring the coffin into the house until he dies.¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁸ Cf. Rashi to M.Ḳ. 22b (Sonc. ed., p. 140). R. Meir of Rothenberg explains the word ḥoleẓin
¹⁹ These things should be avoided so as not to frighten the dying person.


###### Tractate Semachot 1:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:6)


אין משמעין עליו ואין משננין עליו את מעשיו רבי יהודה אומר אם חכם הוא משננין [עליו] את מעשיו:


We do not summon the people²⁰ (cf. Sanh. 47a, Sonc. ed., p. 311), ‘We do not summon the people of the townships’. or recount his good deeds. R. Judah said: If he is a scholar we recount his good deeds.²¹

footnotes:
²⁰ Cf. 1 Sam. 15, 4, XXIII, 8 for this usage of the Heb. verb. Brüll adds the word ‘ayaroth
²¹ Because he is accustomed to have his praises sung and it would not distress him in the circumstances.


###### Tractate Semachot 1:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:7)


אין שואלין בעיר זה את זה בד"א בכפר קטן אבל בעיר גדול מותר מחותך מסורס נפלים ובן שמונה חי בן תשעה מת אין מתעסקין עמו בכל דבר:


²² In the town people do not greet each other.²³ This applies only in a village,²⁴ but in a large city it is permissible.
We do not carry out the mourning rites for one who came out [at birth] by pieces or with feet foremost, or for one born prematurely²⁵, a child who dies within thirty days of birth. at eight months²⁶ alive or at nine months dead.

footnotes:
²² This Rule is not connected with the preceding, but speaks of a case where there is a dead person in the town.
²³ Because all must share in the mourning, and greeting is not permitted to a mourner.
²⁴ Where all learn quickly of the death.
²⁵ Heb. nefel
²⁶ And cannot therefore survive.


###### Tractate Semachot 1:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:8)


נכרי ועבד אין מתעסקין עמו לכל דבר אבל קורין עליו הוי ארי ארי הוי גבור רבי יהודה אומר הוי טבי הוי נאמן אוכל מעמלו אמרו לו מה הנחת לכשרים אמר להן אם היה כשר מפני מה אין קורין עליו: אין מקבלין תנחומין על העבדים ועל השפחות:


For a non-Jew or a slave we do not occupy ourselves with his funeral rites, but we exclaim of him, ‘Alas lion!²⁷ alas mighty man!’. R. Judah said: [We, for instance, exclaim], ‘Alas, Ṭabi!²⁸ alas, faithful man who ate²⁹ of his labour!’. They said to him,³⁰ ‘[If so], what have you left [to be said] of worthy men [who are not slaves]?’ He replied, ‘If he was worthy, why should we not say it of him, since³¹ we do not receive condolence for [the loss of] male or female slaves?’

footnotes:
²⁷ So H; V repeats ‘lion’.
²⁸ The faithful slave of Rabban Gamaliel (cf. Ber. 16b, Sonc. ed. p. 96).
²⁹ In Ber. ‘who worked for his living’.
³⁰ To R. Jose who, in Ber. (Sonc. ed., p. 97), suggested the praise to be made over a faithful slave.
³¹ So H; V lacks ‘since’.


###### Tractate Semachot 1:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:9)


ומעשה כשמתה שפחתו של ר׳ אליעזר נכנסו תלמידיו לנחמו נכנס מפניהם לחצר נכנסו אחריו נכנס לביתו נכנסו אחריו אמר להם כמדומה הייתי שאתם נכוין בפושרין והלא אין אתם נכוין אפילו ברותחין הלא אמרו שאין מקבלין תנחומין על העבדים שהעבדים חשובים כבהמה:


It is related that when R. Eliezer’s female slave died, his disciples came to comfort him. He entered the courtyard [to avoid them], but they followed him; he went into the house, but they followed him. He said to them, ‘I imagined that you would be scalded with tepid water, but now you are not even scalded with hot water.³² Have [the Sages] not said: ³³We do not receive condolence for slaves because they are reckoned as animals?’³⁴

footnotes:
³² The meaning is, I made it clear to you that I did not want to receive your condolence; cf. Ber. loc. cit.
³³ In Ber., ‘Have I not taught you’, etc.
³⁴ The text in Ber. reads: What do we say over them? As we say to a man on the death of his ox or ass, ‘May the Omnipresent repair thy loss’, so we say to a man on the death of his slaves, ‘May the Omnipresent repair thy loss’.


###### Tractate Semachot 1:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:10)


וכשמת טבי עבדו של רבן גמליאל קבל עליו תנחומים אמרו לו לא למדתנו רבינו שאין מקבלין תנחומין על העבדים אמר להן אין טבי עבדי כשאר כל העבדים כשר היה:


And when Ṭabi the slave of Rabban Gamaliel died, he received condolence for him. They asked him, ‘Master, have you not taught us that we do not receive condolence for slaves?’ He replied, ‘My slave Ṭabi was unlike all other slaves; he was a worthy man’.


###### Tractate Semachot 1:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:11)


אף הוא הניח לו להניח תפלין אמרו לו לא למדתנו רבינו שהעבדים פטורין מן התפלין אמר להן אין טבי עבדי כשאר כל העבדים כשר הוא:


He also permitted Ṭabi to put on the *tefillin*. They said to him, ‘Master, have you not taught us that slaves are exempt from [the precept of] *tefillin?*’ He replied, ‘My slave Ṭabi was unlike all other slaves; he was a worthy man’.


###### Tractate Semachot 1:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:12)


אין קורין לעבדים ולשפחות אבא פלוני ואימא פלונית של בית רבן גמליאל היו קורין לאבא טבי ולאימא טביתא:


We do not call male and female slaves ‘Father So-and-so, Mother So-and-so’. Those of the house of Rabban Gamaliel, however, were called ‘Father Ṭabi’ and ‘Mother Ṭabitha’.³⁵ [The female slave Ṭabitha is mentioned in j.Nid. I, 4, 49b.]

footnotes:
³⁵ Quoted from Ber. loc. cit.


###### Tractate Semachot 1:13

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/1:13)


אין קורין לאבות אבינו אלא לשלשה האבות ולא לאימהות אמנו אלא לארבע האימהות:


We confine the term ‘our father’ to the three Patriarchs,³⁶ and the term ‘our mother’ to the four Matriarchs.³⁷

footnotes:
³⁶ Abraham, Isaac and Jacob.
³⁷ Sara, Rebekah, Rachel and Leah. This Rule is also culled, in a slightly different form from Ber. loc. cit.



## Chapter 2

###### Tractate Semachot 2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:1)


המאבד עצמו לדעת אין מתעסקין עמו בכל דבר רבי ישמעאל אומר קורין עליו הוי נטלה הוי נטלה א"ל ר"ע הנח לו בסתמו אל תכבדהו ואל תקללהו אין קורעין עליו ואין חולצין עליו ולא מספידין עליו אבל עומדין עליו בשורה ואומרין עליו ברכת אבלים מפני שהוא כבוד חיים כללו של דבר כל שהוא כבוד של חיים מתעסקין בו כל שאין כבוד של חיים אין הרבים מתעסקין עמו לכל דבר:


We do not occupy ourselves in any respect with the funeral rites of one who committed suicide wilfully. R. Ishmael said: We exclaim over him, ‘Alas for a lost¹, ‘[a life] carried away’. H reads *nishlah* with a similar meaning (cf. *God takth away* [*yeshel*] *his soul*, Job 27, 8). GRA emends to *nithlah*, ‘hanged’. [life!] Alas for a [lost] life!’ R. ‘Aḳiba said to him, ‘Leave him unmourned; speak neither well nor ill of him’.²
We do not rend garments for him,³ Y.D., §345, near relatives do not rend their garments. bare the shoulder, or deliver a memorial address over him. We do, however, stand in a row⁴ for him and recite the benediction of mourners⁵, p. 119. for him, from respect of the living [relatives]. The general rule is: With anything that makes for respect of the living we occupy ourselves, but with anything that does not make for the respect of the living, the public do not in any way occupy themselves.

footnotes:
¹ Heb. niṭṭelah
² lit. ‘do not bless him and do not curse him’.
³ According to the Ṭur
⁴ At the cemetery after the funeral those present divide themselves into two rows and the mourners pass between them to receive condolences.
⁵ The condolence ended with the words, ‘Blessed be He Who comforteth the mourners’ (Keth. 8b, Sonc. ed., p. 40). Hence the term ‘benediction of mourners’; cf. J. Rabbinowitz, Mishnah Megillah


###### Tractate Semachot 2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:2)


איזהו המאבד עצמו לדעת לא שעלה בראש האילן ונפל ומת בראש הגג ונפל ומת אלא זה שאמר הריני עולה לראש הגג או לראש האילן ואפיל עצמי ואמות ורואין אותו שעלה לראש האילן ונפל ומת הרי זה בחזקת המאבד את עצמו בדעת וכל המאבד את עצמו לדעת אין מתעסקין עמו בכל דבר:


Who [comes within the category of] ‘one who committed suicide wilfully’? He does not who climbed to the top of a tree and fell down and died, or he who went up to the top of the roof and fell down and died.⁶ But he who calls out, ‘Look, I am going to the top of the roof or to the top of the tree, and I will throw myself down that I may die’ [comes within the category]. When people saw him go up to the top of a tree or roof [for the purpose] and he fell down and died, he is presumed to have committed suicide wilfully; and with the person who commits suicide wilfully we do not occupy ourselves in any respect.

footnotes:
⁶ It may have been an accidental fall.


###### Tractate Semachot 2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:3)


מצאהו חנוק ותלוי באילן הרוג ומושלך על גבי הסייף הרי זה בחזקת המאבד את עצמו שלא בדעת ואין מונעין הימנו כל דבר:


If a person was found strangled or hanging from a tree or lying dead on a sword, he is presumed not to have committed suicide wilfully, and none [of the rites] are withheld from him.


###### Tractate Semachot 2:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:4)


מעשה בבנו של גורנוס בלוד שברח מבית הספר והראה לו אביו באזנו ונתירא מאביו והלך ואבד עצמו בבגד ובאו ושאלו את רבי טרפון ואמר אין מונעין הימנו כל דבר:


It is related of the son of Gorgias⁷ of Lydda that he ran away from school and his father pointed to his ear.⁸ In fear of his father he went and destroyed himself in a pit.⁹ They went and inquired of R. Ṭarfon who ruled: We do not withhold anything from him.¹⁰

footnotes:
⁷ A variant is Gornos. [He is not mentioned elsewhere in Rabbinic literature.]
⁸ i.e. he threatened to punish him.
⁹ So GRA and H. V wrongly ‘with a garment’.
¹⁰ He is buried with full rites.


###### Tractate Semachot 2:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:5)


מעשה בתינוק אחד מבני ברק ששבר צלוחית בשבת והראה לו אביו באזנו ונתירא מאביו והלך ואבד עצמו בבור ושאלו את רבי עקיבא ואמר אין מונעין הימנו כל דבר:


It is related of a child in Benë Beraḳ¹¹ that he broke a bottle on the Sabbath and his father pointed to his ear. In fear of his father he went and destroyed himself in a pit. They inquired of R. ‘Aḳiba who ruled: We do not withhold anything from him.

footnotes:
¹¹ A town near Jaffa.


###### Tractate Semachot 2:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:6)


מכאן אמרו חכמים אל יראה אדם לתינוק באזנו אלא מלקיהו מיד או ישתוק ולא יאמר לו כלום ר׳ שמעון בן אלעזר אומר יצר תינוק ואשה תהא שמאל דוחה וימין מקרבת:


Hence the Sages ruled: A man should not point at the ear of a child, but either punish him immediately or keep silent and say nothing to him.
R. Simeon b. Eleazar said:¹² With human nature,¹³, which means human instinct; cf. Lazarus, *Ethics of Judaism*, II, pp. 106f. a child and a woman, the left hand should repulse them but the right hand bring them back.¹⁴

footnotes:
¹² Sanh. 107b (Sonc. ed., p. 736), Soṭah 47a (Sonc. ed., p. 246).
¹³ Heb. Yeẓer
¹⁴ One should not be too severe with them, not suppressing their desires but disciplining them.


###### Tractate Semachot 2:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:7)


הרוגי בית דין אין מתעסקין עמהן בכל דבר אחיהם וקרוביהם באין ושואלין את שלום העדים ואת שלום הדיינין שאין בלבנו עליכם שדין אמת דנתם ולא היו מתאבלין אלא אוננים שאין אנינות אלא בלב:


With those executed by a Court we do not occupy ourselves in any respect. Their brothers and relatives come and greet the witnesses and judges¹⁵ [as if to say], ‘We have no [ill feelings] against you in our hearts because you gave a true judgment’. They observe no [public] mourning¹⁶ for them but grieve, since grief is felt only in the heart.

footnotes:
¹⁵ Cf. Sanh. 46a (Sonc. ed., p. 305).
¹⁶ i.e. the seven days, thirty days and the twelve months.


###### Tractate Semachot 2:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:8)


רבי נתן אומר אין בין שתיקה לבכיה כלום:


R. Nathan said: There is no difference between silence and mourning.¹⁷

footnotes:
¹⁷ i.e. the silence of the mourners is itself a form of mourning. Hence the persons mentioned in the previous Rule must not even grieve. It is suggested that R. Nathan’s statement was originally a marginal note which found its way into the text.


###### Tractate Semachot 2:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:9)


אין מברין עליהם שנא׳ (ויקרא יט) לא תאכלו על הדם ב"ד שהרג לא היה טועם כל אותו היום ומניחין אותן לדבר עם אחיהם ועם קרוביהם ובשביל שלא להשהות ומשקין אותן יין ולבונה כדי שלא יצטערו ומלמדין אותן להתוודות שכל המתודה יש לו חלק לעולם הבא שכן מצינו בעכן שאמר לו יהושע (יהושע ז) בני שים נא כבוד לה׳ (אלהיך) [אלהי ישראל] ותן לו תודה והגד נא לי מה עשית אל תכחד ממני ויען עכן את יהושע ויאמר אמנה אנכי חטאתי לה' אלהי ישראל וכזאת וכזאת עשיתי ומה ת"ל וכזאת וכזאת מלמד שמעל בחרם שני פעמים אנכי חטאתי אנכי ולא ביתי אנכי ולא בני מלמד שהתודה באמונה ומניין שכיפר לו וידויו שנאמר ויאמר יהושע מה עכרתנו יעכרך ה׳ ביום הזה ביום הזה אתה עכור ואי אתה עכור לעולם הבא ואומר (דה"א ב) ובני זרח זמרי ואיתן [והימן] וכלכל ודרע כלם חמשה וכי אין אנו יודעים שכולם ה׳ אלא מלמד שאף עכן עמהם לעולם הבא (כיוצא בו הגונב את המכס ואת החרם הרי זה שופך דמים ולא שופך דמים בלבד אלא כאילו עובד עבודת כוכבים ומגלה עריות ומחלל שבתות):


The ‘meal of comfort’¹⁸]’; cf. 2 Sam. 3, 35, *to cause David to eat bread* (*lehabroth*). It was prepared by friends and consisted of lentils and eggs (cf. Sanh. 63a, Sonc. ed., p. 430, n. 6) the round shape of which being a reminder of the revolving wheel of fortune. is not eaten in their case, as it is stated, *Ye shall not eat* [anything] *with the blood*.¹⁹
[The judges of] a Court who ordered the execution of a man used not to eat anything on that day.²⁰
[The condemned] are allowed to converse with their brothers and relatives; and not to delay matters²¹ they are given to drink wine containing frankincense²² so that they should not feel grieved. They are urged²³ to confess, because he who confesses has a portion in the World to Come. We find it so with Achan to whom Joshua said, *My son, give, I pray thee, glory to the Lord, the God of Israel, and make confession unto Him; and tell me now what thou hast done; hide nothing from me. And Achan answered Joshua, and said: Of a truth I have sinned against the Lord, the God of Israel, and thus and thus have I done*.²⁴ What is the meaning of *and thus and thus?* It teaches that he violated the ban twice.²⁵ [44*b*] *I have sinned*, i.e. I and not my household, I and not my children. This teaches that he truthfully²⁶) is explained as *be’emunah* (‘truthfully’). confessed. And whence do we know that his confession made atonement for him? As it is stated, *And Joshua said: Why hast thou troubled us? The Lord shall trouble thee this day*²⁷. 25.—*this day* art thou to be troubled but thou shalt not be troubled in the World to Come; and it states, *And the sons of Zerah: Zimri, and Ethan, and Heman, and Calcol and Dara: five of them in all.*²⁸ Do we not know that there were *five of them in all?*²⁹ It teaches that Achan will be with them in the world to come.³⁰
Similarly one who steals the tax³¹ or anything devoted [to the Sanctuary] is as if he shed blood; and not only is he as if he shed blood, but he is also as if he worships idols, was guilty of immorality and desecrates the Sabbath.³², p. 198n.]

footnotes:
¹⁸ The first meal partaken by the mourners on their return from the funeral is termed ‘the meal of comfort [habra’ah
¹⁹ Lev. 19, 26. This is homiletically interpreted to mean: where blood is shed (i.e. an execution has taken place) no ceremonial meal of mourners should be eaten. Cf. Sanh. loc. cit.
²⁰ Sanh. loc. cit.
²¹ i.e. to avoid giving the appearance that the execution of justice is unduly protracted.
²² To numb the senses; cf. Sanh. 43a (Sonc. ed., p. 279).
²³ lit. ‘they teach them’.
²⁴ Josh. 7, 19f.
²⁵ By taking from the devoted things on two occasions. Sanh. 43b (Sonc. ed., p. 284) explains that he took of the ban three times, twice in the days of Moses and once at Jericho.
²⁶ Of a truth (’omnam
²⁷ ibid
²⁸ 1 Chron. 2, 6.
²⁹ Since five names are specified.
³⁰ Cf. Sanh. 44b (Sonc. ed., p. 291). Zimri, according to a tradition, is identical with Achan. As Achan is mentioned together with the other four, who are considered to be worthy men, it is an indication that his confession was accepted and with them he shared in the World to Come.
³¹ i.e. eludes the customs; cf. B.Ḳ. 113a (Sonc. ed., p. 663), Tosiftha B.Ḳ. X, 8.
³² [On this passage, cf. Büchler, Studies in Sin and Atonement


###### Tractate Semachot 2:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:10)


כל הפורש מדרכי צבור אין מתעסקין עמו בכל דבר אחיהם וקרוביהן לובשין לבנים ומתעטפין לבנים ואוכלין ושותין ושמחים שנאבד שונאו של מקום שנא׳ (תהילים קל״ט:כ״א-כ״ב) הלא משנאיך ה׳ אשנא ובתקוממיך אתקוטט (מפני מה ש) תכלית שנאה שנאתים לאויבים היו לי:


We do not occupy ourselves with those who separated themselves from the community³³, I, 5, cf. *Ṭur* Y.D., §345) the reference is to those who discarded all the religious precepts and ceremonies of Judaism. in any respect. Their brothers and relatives clothe themselves in white, wrap themselves in white and eat and drink and rejoice because an enemy of the All-present perished; as it is stated, *Do not I hate them, O Lord, that hate Thee? And do not I strive with those that rise up against Thee? I hate them with utmost hatred; I count them mine enemies*.³⁴

footnotes:
³³ According to Maimonides (Hil. ’Ebel
³⁴ Ps. 139, 21f.


###### Tractate Semachot 2:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:11)


הרוגי מלכות אין מונעין מהן כל דבר מאימתי מתחילין למנות להם משעה שנתייאשו מלשאול אבל לא מלגנוב שכל הגונב הרי זה שופך דמים ולא שופך דמים בלבד אלא כאילו עובד עבודת כוכבים וכו׳:


We do not withhold mourning rites from those executed by the Government.³⁵
When do we begin to count [the days of mourning] for them?³⁶ From the time that [the relatives] despaired in their appeal [for the body to be delivered to them for burial], but not [given up the hope] of stealing it. For he who steals is as if he shed blood, and not only is he as if he had shed blood, but also as if he worshipped idols, etc.³⁷

footnotes:
³⁵ i.e. the occupying power, viz. the Romans.
³⁶ Usually this begins from the moment the grave is filled in, but here the body of the victim was not handed over to the family for burial.
³⁷ This sentence is repeated from the last clause of Rule 9 and has no relevance to the present context.


###### Tractate Semachot 2:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:12)


מי שנפל לים מי ששטפו נהר מי שאכלתו חיה רעה אין מונעין מהן כל דבר מאימתי מתחילין למנות להם משעה שנתייאשו לבקש מצאוהו אברים אברים אין מונין לו עד שימצא ראשו ורובו רבי יהודה אומר השדרה והגלגולת הן רובו:


Mourning rites are not withheld in any respect from one who fell into the sea, or was washed away by a stream, or killed by a wild beast. When do we begin to count [the days of mourning] for such as these? From the time that hope of finding them has been given up. If they found a body limb by limb, we do not count [the days of mourning] until the head and greater part [of the torso] is found. R. Judah said: The spine and skull constitute the greater part [of the torso].


###### Tractate Semachot 2:13

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:13)


מי שהיה בעלה צלוב עמה בעיר אשתו צלובה עמו בעיר אביו ואמו צלובין עמו בעיר לא ישרה באותה העיר אא"כ היתה עיר גדולה כאנטוכיא לא ישרה בצד זה אלא ישרה בצד זה עד מתי הוא אסור עד שיכלה הבשר כולו ואין הצורה ניכרת בעצמות:


If one’s husband, wife, father or mother had been impaled³⁸ in his city, one should not continue to reside there³⁹ unless it is a city as big as Antioch.⁴⁰ [Even then] one should not reside in the same side of the city but in another part. Until when is one so forbidden? Until the flesh is completely decomposed and the identity⁴¹ unrecognizable from the bones.

footnotes:
³⁸ By the Roman authorities. Impaling was not a form of execution carried out by the Jews.
³⁹ On seeing him, people will recall the impaling of his relative, and that would be a slight on the memory of the victim.
⁴⁰ The ancient capital of Syria. Since it is a large city, the fact might not be generally known.
⁴¹ lit. ‘the form’.


###### Tractate Semachot 2:14

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:14](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/2:14)


(ב"ש אומרים) המגרש את אשתו לא ישרה עמה לא במבוי ולא בחצר אם היה מבוי של שניהם הופך פתחו לצד אחד ואם היתה חצר של שניהם מפנה זה מפני זה ומפנה אשה מפני איש בד"א בזמן שנשאו והכהנת אע"ג שלא נשאו בד"א מן הנשואין אבל מן האירוסין לא וכן החולץ ליבמתו לא יפנה מפני שאין לבו גס בה:


[Beth Shammai said:]⁴² If a man divorced his wife, he should not live in the same alley or courtyard as she.⁴³ If the alley belonged to both of them, he transfers the door [of his house] to another side; and if the courtyard belonged to both of them, one vacates it on account of the other, and the woman vacates it on account of the man. This rule only applies if she has remarried⁴⁴ and in the case of the [divorced] wife of a *kohen* even if she has not remarried.⁴⁵ may not marry a divorced woman, not even his divorced wife (Lev. 21, 7). It further applies only if she is divorced after marriage and not after betrothal.⁴⁶ Likewise one who has freed his sister-in-law from the levirate marriage⁴⁷ does not vacate on account of her, because he does not feel attracted to her.

footnotes:
⁴² GRA omits. For what follows, cf. Keth. 27b, 28a (Sonc. ed., p. 152).
⁴³ Lest he be tempted to cohabit with her.
⁴⁴ So GRA; V ‘they have married’. After remarriage with another she can never return to her first husband (cf. Deut. 24, 3f).
⁴⁵ A kohen
⁴⁶ Cf. Kallah 1, n. 1.
⁴⁷ Cf. Deut. 25, 5ff.



## Chapter 3

###### Tractate Semachot 3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/3:1)


בן יומו שמת הרי הוא לאביו ולאמו כחתן שלם לא סוף דבר בן יומו חי אלא אפי׳ יוצא ראשו ורובו בחיים אלא שדברו חכמים בהווה:


A child who lived only one day counts to his father and mother as a fully-grown man.¹, lit. ‘a complete bridegroom’, is based on *ḥathan damim, a bridegroom of blood* (Ex. 4, 25). For the idea that circumcision makes one ‘complete’, cf. Ned. 31b (Sonc. ed., p. 93). [The words] ‘who lived only one day’ are not to be taken as an [irreducible] limit;² even if only his head and the greater part of his body came out viable [he is so counted, but in their formulation of the law] the Sages speak of what actually happens.

footnotes:
¹ With respect to mourning; cf. Nid. 44a (Sonc. ed., p. 303). The phrase ḥathan shalem
² lit. ‘the end of the matter’.


###### Tractate Semachot 3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/3:2)


ויוצא בחיק נקבר באשה אחת ובשני אנשים אבא שאול אומר באיש א׳ ובשתי נשים אמרו לו אשה אחת מתייחדת עם שני אנשים ואין איש אחד מתייחד עם שתי נשים אין עומדין עליו בשורה ואין אומרין עליו ברכת אבלים עד שיהא בן שלשים יום ובן שלשים יום יוצא באנשים ובנשים ובדלוסקמא ובאגפים עד בן שנים עשר חדש בן שנים עשר חדש יוצא באנשים ובנשים ובדלוסקמא ובכתף עד בן שלש שנים רבי יהודה אומר אם רצה אביו לכבדו מוציאין לו ארון לבית הקברות עד בן שלש שנים:


³ [An infant] who is carried in the arms⁴ is buried by one woman and two men.⁵ Abba Saul said: [The burial may be done] by one man and two women. [The Sages] said to him: One woman may be alone with two men, but one man may not be alone with two women.⁶
[The people at the burial] do not stand in line on account of him and they need not recite the mourner’s benediction on account of him unless he was thirty days old. If he was thirty days old, he is accompanied [to the grave] by men and women and is carried in a case upon the arms⁷ up to the age of twelve months. If he was twelve months old, he is accompanied by men and women and is carried in a case on the shoulder up to the age of three years. R. Judah said: Should his father desire to honour him, they take him out in a coffin to the cemetery even before⁸ he is three years old.

footnotes:
³ Cf. M.Ḳ. 24a (Sonc. ed., p. 151).
⁴ lit. ‘goes out in the bosom’.
⁵ For reasons of propriety. GRA inserts ‘but not by one man and two women’.
⁶ A quotation from Ḳid. IV, 12 (Sonc. ed., p. 413).
⁷ Cf. M.Ḳ. 24b (Sonc. ed., pp. 151f). Apparently not a full-sized coffin is meant.
⁸ So GRA; V ‘until’.


###### Tractate Semachot 3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/3:3)


בן שלש שנים יוצא במטה רבי עקיבא אומר הוא בן שלש שנים ואיבריו כבן שתים (אינו יוצא במטה) הוא בן שתים ואיבריו בבן שלש יוצא במטה שמעון בן אחי עזריה אומר כל היוצא במטה מקלסין לפניו רבי מאיר אומר משום רבי אלעזר בן עזריה כל שהרבים מכירין אותו מתעסקין עמו וכל שאין הרבים מכירין אותו אין הרבים מתעסקין עמו :


If three years old he is taken out on a bier. R. ‘Aḳiba said: If he is three years old but his limbs are [developed] like those of an infant two years old,⁹ or if he is two years old but his limbs are like those of an infant three years old, he is carried on a bier. Simeon the brother of Azariah said: For anyone who is taken out on a bier the people show their distress.¹⁰ which in Rabbinic Heb. means ‘stamp with the foot’ as a sign of mourning (cf. Ezek. 6, 11).] The Talmud, which gives the name of the Rabbi as Simeon b. Eleazar, uses a different verb; cf. M.Ḳ. 24b (Sonc. ed., p. 151, n. 9). R. Meir said in the name of R. Eleazar b. Azariah: If he is known to the public at large they participate in connection with him [at the funeral], but if not, they do not participate in connection with him.

footnotes:
⁹ V wrongly in brackets ‘he is not carried on a bier’.
¹⁰ [Heb. meḳallesin


###### Tractate Semachot 3:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/3:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/3:4)


רבי יהודה משום רבי אלעזר בן עזריה אומר אף שכיניו לענין הספד מספידין לעניים בני שלש ולעשירים בני ארבע רבי עקיבא אומר לעניים בני שש ולעשירים בני שבע בני עשירים בבני חכמים בני חכמים כבני מלכים (מתעסקין עמהם):


R. Judah said in the name of R. Eleazar b. Azariah: For the purpose of a memorial address it is sufficient that his neighbours [know him]. In the case of the poor a memorial address is delivered for a child of three,¹¹ and in the case of the rich for a child of four. R. ‘Aḳiba said: In the case of the poor, for a child of six and in the case of the rich for a child of seven. The children of the rich are like the children of scholars and the children of scholars like the children of kings.¹²

footnotes:
¹¹ The child being a greater joy to poor parents who have few pleasures in life, and so his death is a greater loss.
¹² The children are praised in the memorial address to honour the parents.


###### Tractate Semachot 3:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/3:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/3:5)


תינוק שיודע לישא וליתן יוצא במעשה עצמו אם אין לו מעשים יוצא במעשה אבותיו ואם אין לאבותיו מעשים יוצא במעשה קרוביו הכלה יוצאת בין בכבוד אביה בין בכבוד חמיה מפני (שמעלין בכבוד ולא מורידין) לא מוסיפין בתחילה אבל מוסיפין על העיקר:


In the case of a child of developed intelligence¹³ we dwell [in the memorial address for him] on his own [good] deeds, but if he has no such deeds we dwell on the deeds of his parents; and if his parents have no such deeds we dwell on the deeds of his relatives. In the case of a bride we dwell on the honour of her father or father-in-law, because she rises [in esteem] with him¹⁴ but does not fall with him. We may not ascribe merits to one who has none,¹⁵ but we may magnify those which he possessed.¹⁶

footnotes:
¹³ lit. ‘he knows how to transact [his affairs]’.
¹⁴ viz. her father-in-law. The reading of GRA and H is adopted.
¹⁵ lit. ‘we do not add from the start’. Cf. Ber. 62a (Sonc. ed., p. 390), ‘As the dead are called to account [for their deeds], so are funeral orators [who exaggerate the merits of the dead]’.
¹⁶ lit. ‘we may add to the stock’.


###### Tractate Semachot 3:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/3:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/3:6)


בירושלים היו אומרים עשה לפני מיטתך וביהודה היו אומרים עשה לאחר מיטתך (של) שבירושלים לא היו אומרים לפני מיטתו של מת אלא דבריו של מת וביהודה היו אומרים דברים שיש בו ודברים שאין בו המהלכין אחר המטה לא היו עונין אחריהם ואומרים אלא דברים שיש בו (ודברים שאין בו). מבן שלש ועד בן שלשים יוצא כחתן מבן שלשים עד בן ארבעים יוצא כאח מבן ארבעים ועד בן חמשים יוצא כאב:


¹⁷ In Jerusalem there was a saying, ‘Perform actions [which can be lamented] in front of your bier’,¹⁸ and in Judea there was a saying, ‘Perform actions [which can be lamented] behind your bier’. For in Jerusalem they recounted in front of a dead man’s bier only the merits which he possessed; but in Judea they recounted merits which he possessed and did not possess. They, however, who walked behind the bier only repeated after them the merits which he possessed.¹⁹
[One who dies] between the ages of three and thirty [is mourned] like a bridegroom;²⁰ between thirty and forty like a brother;²¹ between forty and fifty like a father.²²

footnotes:
¹⁷ Cf. Shab. 153a (Sonc. ed., p. 781).
¹⁸ Apparently the place of the people in a funeral procession differed according to local custom. In Jerusalem they walked in front of the bier, in Judea behind it. Cf. Tosiftha Pes. II, 16.
¹⁹ The emended text of GRA and H is followed.
²⁰ Since he may be unmarried.
²¹ He may be married but childless.
²² As one mourned by his children.


###### Tractate Semachot 3:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/3:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/3:7)


רשב"א אומר מבן שלשים ועד בן ארבעים אם יש לו בנים או לרוב בני גילו בנים יוצא כאב ואם לאו יוצא כאח מבן מ׳ ועד בן נ׳ אם יש לו בני בנים או לרוב בני גילו בנים יוצא כאב ואם לאו יוצא כאח:


R. Simeon b. Eleazar said: Between thirty and forty, if he has children or the majority of his own age have children, [he is mourned] like a father; if not, like a brother. Between forty and fifty, if he has children or the majority of his own age have grandchildren, [he is mourned] like a grandfather, if not like a father.²³

footnotes:
²³ So GRA. V and H read: ‘have children [he is mourned] like a father; if not like a brother’.


###### Tractate Semachot 3:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/3:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/3:8)


מת פחות מחמשים מת בהכרת לחמשים ושתים שנה זו היא מיתת של שמואל הרמתי מת לששים שנה זו היא מיתה האמורה בתורה שנאמר (איוב ה׳:כ״ו) תבא בכלח אלי קבר מת לשבעים שנה זו היא מיתה של שיבה שנא׳ (תהילים צ׳:י׳) ימי שנותינו בהם שבעים שנה מת לשמונים שנה זו היא מיתה של גבורה שנא׳ (שם) ואם בגבורות שמונים שנה וכן ברזילי הגלעדי אמר לדוד (שמואל ב י״ט:ל״ו) בן שמנים שנה אנכי היום האדע בין טב לרע (יתר מכאן חיים של צער):


**²⁴ If one dies under fifty, that is death by *kareth;*²⁵, p. 54. at fifty-two, that is the death of Samuel of Ramah; at sixty, that is the death referred to in the Torah, as it is stated, *Thou shalt come to thy grave in ripe age;*²⁶ is בכלח in Heb., the numerical value of the letters being sixty. Cf. M.Ḳ. *loc. cit.* at seventy, that is the death of the hoary head, as it is stated, *The days of our years are threescore years and ten;*²⁷ at eighty, that is the death of a vigorous old man,²⁸ as it is stated, *Or even by strength fourscore years*.²⁹. More than this is a life of trouble, for so³⁰ *Barzilai the Gileadite said to David, I am this day fourscore years old; can I discern between good and bad?*³¹

footnotes:
²⁴ Cf. M.Ḳ. 28a (Sonc. ed., p. 182). In j.Bik. II, 1, 64C there are variants in the enumeration.
²⁵ Cf. J. Rabbinowitz, Mishnah Megillah
²⁶ Job 5, 26; in ripe age
²⁷ Ps. 90, 10.
²⁸ lit. ‘of strength’.
²⁹ ibid
³⁰ This is GRA’s reading; V and H have ‘and likewise Barzilai’ and insert ‘more than this is a life of trouble’ after the quotation.
³¹ 2 Sam. 19, 36.


###### Tractate Semachot 3:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/3:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/3:9)


המת ליום א׳ מיתה של זעף. המת לשנים מיתה של בהלה. המת לשלשה מיתה של מגפה. לארבעה לחמשה מיתה חטופה. המת לששה מיתה האמורה בתורה. המת לשבע מיתה של חיבה. יתר מכאן של יסורין:


If one dies after [an illness of] one day, that is a death by [divine] wrath;³² after two days, it is a precipitous death; after three days, it is a death by pestilence; after four days, it is a hurried death; after six days, it is the death decreed in the Torah;³³ has ‘five’ instead of ‘six’, quotes Deut. 31, 14, *Behold thy days approach that thou must die* and explains: *behold* accounts for one (cf. Sonc. ed., *loc. cit.*, n. 6), *thy days* accounts for two more, *approach* gives a further two, which makes five. after seven days, it is a death of [divine] love; after a longer period, it is a death of suffering.

footnotes:
³² In M.Ḳ. 28a (Sonc. ed., p. 181) the reading is ‘a death by being hustled away’.
³³ A normal death. M.Ḳ. loc. cit.


###### Tractate Semachot 3:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/3:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/3:10)


רבי חנינא בן אנטיגנוס אומר זקן שאכל את החלב או שחלל את השבת מת בהכרת וכי מה מודיענו שמיתתם בהכרת אלא שמת לשלשה מת בהכרת לארבעה מיתה חטופה המת לששה מיתה האמורה בתורה וכו׳. א"ר יהודה חסידים הראשונים היו מתייסרין בחולי מעיים כעשרים יום קודם מיתתן כדי למרק את הכל כדי שיבואו זכאין לעתיד לבא שנא׳ (משלי כ״ז:כ״א) מצרף לכסף וכור לזהב ואיש לפי מהללו:


R. Ḥanina b. Antigonos said: An old man who eats forbidden fat³⁴ or one who desecrates the Sabbath dies by *kareth*. And what is there to show us that they died by *kareth?* But he who dies after [an illness of] three days dies by *kareth;* after four by a hurried death; after six, it is the death decreed in the Torah; after seven it is a death of [divine] love; after a longer period it is a death by suffering.
R. Judah said:³⁵ The pious men of former times used to suffer with stomach trouble for about twenty days before death in order to be cleansed of everything, so that they should come pure³⁶ into the World to Come;³⁷ as it is said, *The refining pot is for silver, and the furnace for gold, and a man is tried by his praise*.³⁸. XVII, 3 as the proof-text.] [45*a*].

footnotes:
³⁴ Cf. Lev. 3, 17.
³⁵ Cf. Midrash Rabbah, Genesis, LXVI, 2 (Sonc. ed., p. 549) and Shab. 118b (Sonc. ed., p. 583).
³⁶ So GRA; V and H ‘innocent’.
³⁷ [For the thought, cf. ARN I, 1, p. 1, n. 5.]
³⁸ Prov. 27, 21. [H quotes ibid



## Chapter 4

###### Tractate Semachot 4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:1)


על כל האמור בתורה כהן מטמא על אביו ועל אמו על בנו ועל בתו על אחיו ועל אשתו ועל אחותו רבי עקיבא אומר שניים להם מתאבל ואונן ולא מטמא:


¹ For all [the nearest of kin] for whom a *kohen* defiles himself as commanded in the Torah² [a mourner must observe mourning], viz. for his father, mother, son, daughter, brother, sister and wife.³ R. ‘Aḳiba said: For relatives in the second degree⁴). In Sonc. ed., p. 130, n. 7 the enumeration is grandfather, grandmother, grandchildren and the brothers and sisters of parents. he observes the periods of mourning and grieves,⁵ [but should he be a *kohen*] he does not defile himself.

footnotes:
¹ Cf. M.Ḳ. 20b (Sonc. ed., p. 130).
² Lev. 21, 2f.
³ So GRA and H. V reads ‘wife and sister’.
⁴ i.e. his grandfather, grandchildren and nephews (Rashi to M.Ḳ. loc. cit.
⁵ While waiting for the funeral to take place; cf. Rule 19 below.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:2)


רשב"א אומר אבי אביו ובן בנו וחכמים אומרים כל מי שמתאבל עליו מתאבלין עמו וכל שאין מתאבל עליו אין מתאבלין עמו ספק שהוא אחיו ספק שאינו אחיו ספק שהוא בנו ספק שאינו בנו מתאבל ואונן ואינו מטמא:


R. Simeon b. Eleazar said: He mourns for his father’s father and son’s son.⁶ But the Sages said: For whomsoever he mourns he should mourn with, and for whomsoever he does not mourn, he does not mourn with.⁷ If there is a doubt whether [the deceased] is a brother or not⁸ or whether he is his son or not, he mourns and grieves for him, but [if he is a *kohen*] he does not defile himself for him.

footnotes:
⁶ R. Simeon either interprets that R. ‘Aḳiba excludes the daughter’s son and the sister’s son, or differs from him in that he holds that one mourns only for these two relatives in the second degree.
⁷ As one mourns in sympathy with one’s father on the death of his father, so should a father mourn with his son on the death of the latter’s son.
⁸ Where the identity of the child at birth or in infancy became confused. j.Yeb. XI, 7, 12a cites an actual case.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:3)


אשתו ארוסה לא מתאבל ולא מטמא גרושה אע"פ שיש לו הימנה בנים לא מתאבל ולא אונן ולא מטמא:


For his betrothed wife one does not mourn, grieve,⁹ or [if a *kohen*] defile himself.¹⁰ (Lev. 21, 2). The betrothed woman does not come in this category and the divorced wife has lost that status (cf. Sanh. 28b, Sonc. ed., p. 171). See also Yeb. 22a (Sonc. ed., p. 132), 29b (Sonc. ed., p. 183). For his divorced wife, although he had children by her, he does not mourn, grieve, or [if a *kohen*] defile himself.

footnotes:
⁹ ‘Or grieve’ is added by GRA and H.
¹⁰ The duty of mourning, etc., for a wife devolves upon the husband only when she can be termed his kin


###### Tractate Semachot 4:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:4)


([איזה אנינה מיום מיתה ועד יום קבורה כדברי רבי מאיר וחכמים אומרים אין לך אסור באנינה אלא יום אחד בלבד]) אלמנה לכהן גדול גרושה וחלוצה לכהן הדיוט מתאבל ואונן ולא מטמא:


Which is [the period of] ‘grieving’? From the day of the death until the day of the burial, according to R. Meir; but the Sages said: ‘Grieving’ is only for one day.¹¹
If a High Priest is married to a widow or an ordinary *kohen* to a divorced woman or one freed¹². 2 (priests in general) and *ibid*. XXI, 11 (the High Priest). Cf. Yeb. 90b (Sonc. ed., p. 616), ‘but he may not defile himself for his unlawful wife’. from levirate marriage, he mourns and grieves for her but does not defile himself.

footnotes:
¹¹ Cf. Zeb. 100b (Sonc. ed., pp. 483f).
¹² Although such marriages are forbidden (Lev. 21, 7, 14, the levirate woman being treated in Rabbinic law as a divorced woman), yet if contracted they are valid; but being irregular, they come under the prohibition of ibid


###### Tractate Semachot 4:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:5)


אחותו ארוסה ר"מ אומר מטמא רבי יהודה ור"ש אומרים לא יטמא:


For his betrothed sister, R. Meir said that [a *kohen*] defiles himself. R. Judah and R. Simeon said that he does not.¹³, betrothal being a form of marriage; cf. Yeb. 60a (Sonc. ed., pp. 400f).

footnotes:
¹³ Lev. 21, 3, and for his sister a virgin


###### Tractate Semachot 4:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:6)


מוכת עץ ר"י אומר יטמא ור"מ ור"ש אומרים לא יטמא באנוסה ומפותה הכל מודים שלא יטמא בבוגרת הכל מודים שיטמא:


For [his sister who was] ‘wounded’,¹⁴ R. Judah said that [a *kohen*] defiles himself. R. Meir and R. Simeon said that he does not. For a woman who was raped or seduced, all agree that [a *kohen*] does not defile himself. For an adolescent,¹⁵ all agree that he defiles himself.

footnotes:
¹⁴ lit. ‘struck by wood’, viz. one who lost her hymen as the result of a blow; Yeb. loc. cit.
¹⁵ i.e. over the age of twelve years and six months.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:7)


כללו של דבר שהיה רבי שמעון אומר כל שהיא כשירה לכהן גדול מטמא לה כל שאינה כשרה לכהן גדול לא יטמא:


The general principle laid down by R. Simeon is: He defiles himself for any one who is eligible [to marry] a High Priest, but may not defile himself for one who is not eligible [to marry] a High Priest.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:8)


על כל אלה שאמרו כהן מטמא אינו רשות אלא חובה רבי שמעון אומר רשות רבי יהושע אומר חובה:


Of all those regarding whom they said that a *kohen* defiles himself, the defilement is not voluntary but obligatory. R. Simeon said: It is voluntary. R. Joshua said: It is obligatory.¹⁶ [*E.V. may he defile*] *himself* (Lev. 21, 3) is to be understood as an obligation; cf. Zeb. 100a (Sonc. ed., p. 480).

footnotes:
¹⁶ The phrase for her he defiles


###### Tractate Semachot 4:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:9)


מעשה ביוסף הכהן שמתה אשתו בערבי פסחים ולא רצה ליטמא לה ודחפוהו חביריו וטמאוהו על כרחו אמרו לו אינה רשות אלא חובה:


It is related of Joseph, a *kohen*, that his wife died on the eve of Passover and he would not defile himself for her. His colleagues pushed him¹⁷ and defiled him forcibly. Defilement is not voluntary but compulsory.

footnotes:
¹⁷ He forced him to make contact with the corpse; Zeb. loc. cit.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:10)


עד אימתי מטמא לה ר"מ אומר כל אותו היום רבי יהודה אומר עד שלש רבי יהודה אומר משום רבי טרפון עד שיסתם הגולל:


Until how long is it one’s duty to defile himself for her? R. Meir said: The whole of that day;¹⁸ R. Judah said: Up to three hours. R. Judah said in the name of R. Ṭarfon: Until the top stone closes the grave.¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁸ i.e. of the death. The funeral generally took place on the same day.
¹⁹ Cf. Nazir, Sonc. ed., p. 202, n. 5.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:11)


מעשה שמת שמעון בן יהוצדק בלוד ובא יוחנן אחיו מן הגליל [לטמא לו] מאחר שנסתם הגולל לטמא (לאחוריהם) ובאו ושאלו לחכמים ואמרו אל יטמא פתחו לו על הקבר ויראה:


It is related that when R. Simeon b. Yehoẓadaḳ died in Lydda, his brother Joḥanan came from Galilee to defile himself for him after the top stone had closed the grave. They went and inquired of the Sages who said: He may not defile himself, but open the grave for him that he may view [the body].


###### Tractate Semachot 4:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:12)


מעשה בתינוק אחד שכתב נכסיו לאחרים והניח את משפחתו והיו בני משפחתו מעוררין ואומרים יבדק ובאו ושאלו לחכמים ואמרו אל יבדק מאחר שנסתם הגולל אין מזיזין את המת ממקומו ודבר אחר כיון שמת שערו נשתנה:


It is related²⁰ of a boy²¹ that he willed away his property to strangers and left his family out. They protested saying, ‘Let [his body] be examined’.²² They went and inquired of the Sages who said, ‘The body may not be examined; after the top stone has closed the grave, a corpse may not be moved from his place’. Another explanation is: Since he is dead, his hair has undergone change.²³

footnotes:
²⁰ Cf. B.B. 154a (Sonc. ed., p. 669).
²¹ A minor under thirteen years and one day, who could not legally dispose of his property.
²² i.e. let the body be exhumed and his age ascertained from the presence or absence of signs of puberty.
²³ Therefore the examination cannot produce decisive evidence of age.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:13

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:13)


כהן הדיוט שהוא מטמא לקרוביו אל יטמא לאחרים אפילו בו ביום בד"א בזמן שיש בו כדי נושאי המטה וקבריה אבל אין שם כדי נושאי המטה וקבריה ה"ז יטמא נטמא ובאו כדי נושאי המטה וקבריה פורש למקום טהרה:


An ordinary *kohen* who defiled himself for his near kin may not defile himself for strangers even on the same day.²⁴ This rule applies only when there are sufficient to carry the bier and bury him, but if there are not sufficient to carry the bier and bury him, then he must defile himself.²⁵ (v. Glos.). Should he have defiled himself and sufficient people come to carry the bier and bury, he must withdraw to a place of cleanness.

footnotes:
²⁴ Cf. Nazir 42b (Sonc. ed., pp. 154f).
²⁵ Then it is a case of meth miẓwah


###### Tractate Semachot 4:14

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:14](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:14)


היו שם שני דרכים אחת קרובה וטמאה ואחת רחוקה וטהורה אם היה העם הולך לו ברחוקה ילך עמהם ואי לאו ילך בטמאה מפני כבוד השם:


Should there be two roads [for his return from the cemetery] one short but [ritually] unclean and one long but [ritually] clean, if the other persons take the longer road he should go with them; and if they do not, he should take the unclean road out of respect for the people.²⁶

footnotes:
²⁶ This is the reading of H.V has ‘respect for the Name’.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:15

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:15](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:15)


היה עומד וקובר את מתו עד שהוא עומד בתוך הקבר מקבל מאחרים וקובר פירש הרי זה לא יטמא:


While he stands and buries his dead he may, during the time he is standing in the grave, receive [a body] for burial from others;²⁷ but once he has withdrawn from the grave he may not defile himself.

footnotes:
²⁷ As he is engaged in burying his dead, the new burial merely prolongs his uncleanness; cf. Nazir 42b (Sonc. ed., p. 155).


###### Tractate Semachot 4:16

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:16](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:16)


נטמא בו ביום רבי טרפון מחייב ורבי עקיבא פוטר נטמא לאחר אותו היום הכל מודים שהוא חייב מפני שהוא סותר יום אחד:


If he defiles himself [for a stranger] on the same day [as for another], R. Ṭarfon deems him culpable, but R. ‘Aḳiba frees him from culpability. If he defiles himself the next day, all are agreed that he is culpable, because he has rendered one day [of his mourning] void.²⁸

footnotes:
²⁸ i.e. he has to begin afresh the seven days of mourning.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:17

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:17](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:17)


מטמא כהן על הקרובים אפילו הן פסולין בנו ובתו נתינים וממזרים אחיו ואחותו נתינין או ממזרין חוץ ממי שיש לו מן השפחה ומן הנכרית (וחוץ ממי שיש לו ממקום אחר):


A *kohen* must defile himself for his near kin even when they have a disqualification;²⁹ for his son and daughter who are *Nethinim*³⁰ or illegitimate;³¹ for his brother and sister who are *Nethinim* or illegitimate; but not for any issue he may have from a slave or a non-Jewess (nor from the issue he may have from another source).³²

footnotes:
²⁹ If they are his children by marriage to a divorced woman, etc. (cf. Lev. 21, 7).
³⁰ The name given to the descendants of the Gibeonites whom Joshua made hewers of wood and drawers of water (Josh. 9).
³¹ The issue of an incestuous marriage.
³² The clause in brackets is omitted by GRA. It may, however, refer to the issue of one who left the community.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:18

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:18](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:18)


ספק אחיו ספק שאינו אחיו ספק שהוא בנו ספק שאינו בנו מתאבל ואונן ואינו מטמא:


If there is a doubt whether [the deceased] is his brother or not or whether he is his son or not, he mourns and grieves but [if a *kohen*] he does not defile himself.³³

footnotes:
³³ Repeated from the latter part of Rule 2, and Rule 19 is repeated from Rule 4.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:19

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:19](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:19)


איזהו אנינה מיום מיתה עד יום קבורה דברי ר׳ מאיר וחכמים אומרים אין לך (איסור) באנינה אלא יום אחד בלבד:


How long is [the period of] ‘grieving’? From the day of the death until the day of the burial, according to the view of R. Meir; but the Sages said: ‘Grieving’ is for one day only.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:20

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:20](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:20)


כהן גדול שהוא מטמא למתים או שפרע או שפרם הרי זה לוקה ארבעים:


A High Priest who defiles himself for the dead, lets the hair of his head go loose, or rends his garments,³⁴ receives forty lashes.³⁵

footnotes:
³⁴ Cf. Lev. 21, 10.
³⁵ Cf. Tosiftha Mak. IV, 17.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:21

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:21](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:21)


כל טומאה מן המת שהנזיר מגלח עליה סופג את הארבעים ושאין הנזיר מגלח עליה אין סופג את הארבעים:


Any defilement contracted by a Nazirite from the dead and for which he must shave his head,³⁶ if contracted by a High Priest he receives forty lashes for it; but [for those defilements for which] the Nazirite has not to shave his head, [if contracted by a High Priest], he does not receive forty lashes.³⁷

footnotes:
³⁶ Cf. Num. 6, 9.
³⁷ Cf. Nazir 49b (Sonc. ed., p. 184). If, e.g., the Nazirite came in contact with a corpse or the flesh of a corpse of the size of an olive.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:22

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:22](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:22)


(כהן גדול שהוא מטמא או שנכנס לבית הקברות הרי זה לוקה ארבעים):


A High Priest³⁸ who defiles himself or enters a cemetery receives forty lashes.

footnotes:
³⁸ This Rule virtually repeats Rule 20 and is deleted by GRA.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:23

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:23](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:23)


(אע"פ) שנכנסו למדור הנכרים לארץ עממים ולבית הפרס או שיצא חוץ לארץ מלקין אותו מכת מרדות:


If [a *kohen*] entered³⁹ the residence of non-Jews⁴⁰ or the territory of Gentiles⁴¹ or a *beth happeras*⁴² denotes ‘half’ the length of a furrow. A *beth happera* is defined by Jastrow as ‘an area of a square *peras*, especially a field, of a square *peras*, declared unclean on account of crushed bones carried over it from a ploughed grave’. or goes outside the land [of Israel],⁴³ he receives punishment for disobedience.⁴⁴

footnotes:
³⁹ So GRA and H. V reads ‘although they entered’.
⁴⁰ Who do not conform to the laws of uncleanness and he is therefore liable to contract defilement.
⁴¹ i.e. a district in Palestine which is inhabited only by non-Jews.
⁴² The noun peras
⁴³ Where the laws of uncleanness are not observed by the inhabitants.
⁴⁴ Unlike the forty stripes which is the punishment for certain offences, this penalty is left to the discretion of the Court and is consequently a Rabbinical form of punishment.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:24

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:24](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:24)


נכנס לשדה שאבד קבר לתוכה פטור עד שיהלך את כולה:


If he enters a field wherein [the location of] a grave had disappeared, he is not culpable unless he traverses the whole of it.⁴⁵

footnotes:
⁴⁵ Then he must have come in contact with it.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:25

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:25](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:25)


מטמא כהן ויוצא ח"ל לדיני ממונות ולדיני נפשות לקדש את החדש ולעבר את השנה ולהציל את שדהו מנכרי ואפילו יוצא ועורר עליה:


A *kohen* may defile himself by going outside the land [of Israel] for the purpose of [dealing with a non-Jewish Court in] a civil suit or a capital charge, to announce the new moon or intercalate the year, or to rescue his field from a non-Jew, even if he only goes to protest [against his taking possession of it].⁴⁶

footnotes:
⁴⁶ Cf. ‘A.Z. 13a (Sonc. ed., p. 66).


###### Tractate Semachot 4:26

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:26](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:26)


ללמד תורה ולישא אשה רבי יהודה אומר אם אין לו מאין ללמד יטמא [ואם לאו אל יטמא ר"י אומר אע"פ (שאין) [שיש] לו מאין ללמוד יטמא] שלא מכל אדם זכאי ללמוד בתורה מעשה ביוסי הבבלי שנטמא ויצא חוץ לארץ אצל רבי יוסי בציידין:


⁴⁷. [He may go outside the land of Israel] to study Torah or to take a wife. R. Judah said: If he has nowhere else to study he may so defile himself, otherwise he may not. R. Jose said: Even though he has elsewhere to study he may defile himself, because no one is so meritorious as to be able to learn Torah from any man. It is related of Jose the Babylonian that he defiled himself by going outside the land [of Israel to study Torah] with R. Jose in Zidon.⁴⁸

footnotes:
⁴⁷ ibid
⁴⁸ In Phœnicia.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:27

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:27](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:27)


מטמא כהן על עצם כשעורה מאביו ורבי יהודה אומר בתחלה אין אדם מטמא על עצם כשעורה מאביו (אבל חוזר הוא על עצם כשעורה מאביו אמרו לו) לא יטמא כהן על אבר מן החי אפילו מאביו:


⁴⁹ A *kohen* must defile himself for [the burial of] a bone from his father’s [corpse] of the size of a barleycorn,⁵⁰., p. 159, n. 2). but he may not defile himself for a limb severed from a living person be it even that of his father. R. Judah said: At first [a *kohan*] may not defile himself for a limb severed from a corpse, be it even that of his father.

footnotes:
⁴⁹ The text of this Rule is in disorder and the translation follows the text of GRA.
⁵⁰ Cf. Nazir 43b Sonc. ed


###### Tractate Semachot 4:28

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:28](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:28)


מעשה ביוסף פסקס שעלתה נימא ברגליו ונכנס הרופא לחתכה אמרו לו כשתניח לו כחוט השערה חתכה והניח בה כחוט השערה והודיעו קרא לנחוני בנו אמר לו בני ע"כ אתה חייב ליטפל מכאן ואילך צא לך שאמרו חכמים אין אדם מטמא על אבר מן החי (ואפילו) מאביו כשבא הדבר אצל חכמים אמרו על זה נאמר (תהלים קיט) נפשי בכפי תמיד ותורתך לא שכחתי (קהלת ז) יש צדיק אובד בצדקו (ויש רשע מאריך ברעתו):


It is related of Joseph [the son of] Pisḳas⁵¹ who had a swelling on his foot that a doctor came to remove it. He said to him, ‘When you have left as much as a single hair, tell me’. He cut the abscess, and when he had left only as much as a single hair,⁵² he informed him. He called his son Neḥunia and said to him, ‘My son, until now it was your duty to attend to me, but now go your way; for the Sages said, “One must not defile himself for a limb that was severed from a living person, even if it be his father’s” ’.⁵³. When the Sages heard of it they said, ‘Of such a man as he it has been stated, *My soul is continually in my hand; yet have I not forgotten Thy law;*⁵⁴ [and it further states,] *There is a righteous man that perisheth in his righteousness*⁵⁵. *and there is a wicked man that prolongeth his life in his evil-doing.’*⁵⁶

footnotes:
⁵¹ Cf. j.Nazir VII, 1, 55d. The text here is corrupt and the reading of GRA which agrees with the Talmud is followed.
⁵² i.e. the swelling was hanging from the foot as by a hair.
⁵³ Joseph was a kohen
⁵⁴ Ps. 119, 109.
⁵⁵ Although Joseph was in danger of dying under the operation, he remembered to warn his son of the law of defilement which devolved upon a kohen
⁵⁶ Eccl. 7, 15.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:29

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:29](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:29)


מצא מת מצוה הרי זה מטפל בו וקוברו איזהו מת מצוה כל שהוא צווח ואין בני העיר שומעין את קולו אבל בני העיר שומעין את קולו אין זה מת מצוה:


If he found a *meth miẓwah*, he must attend to him and bury him. Which is a *meth miẓwah.?* When [he who finds the body] cries out and the inhabitants of the city cannot hear his voice; but if they can hear his voice, it is not a *meth miẓwah*.⁵⁷

footnotes:
⁵⁷ Yeb. 89b (Sonc. ed., p. 610), Nazir 43b (Sonc. ed., p. 158).


###### Tractate Semachot 4:30

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:30](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:30)


לעולם הוא מת מצוה עד שיהא שם כדי נושאי המטה וקובריה אבל יש שם כדי נושאי המטה וקובריה אין זה מת מצוה:


[The body remains] a *meth miẓwah* until sufficient people arrive to carry the bier and bury him; but once there are sufficient to carry the bier and bury him it is no longer a *meth miẓwah*. [45*b*]


###### Tractate Semachot 4:31

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:31](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:31)


על הכל מטמא כהן גדול נזיר ונשיא רבי אליעזר אומר יטמא כהן ואל יטמא נזיר שזה מביא קרבן טומאה על טומאתו וזה אין מביא קרבן על טומאתו וחכ"א יטמא נזיר ויביא מאה קרבנות ואל יטמא כהן הדיוט שזה קדושתו מרחם וזה אין קדושתו מרחם (מפני ש) זה קדושתו לשעה וזה קדושתו קדושת עולם:


If a High Priest⁵⁸ and a Nazirite [found a *meth miẓwah*], R. Eliezer said that the High Priest should defile himself and not the Nazirite, because the latter must offer a sacrifice on his defilement⁵⁹ whereas the former does not. But the Sages said: The Nazirite should defile himself even if he has to bring a hundred sacrifices, and let not the High Priest⁶⁰’. defile himself, because the one is consecrated from the womb and the other is not,⁶¹ and the consecration of the one is temporary⁶² and that of the other permanent.

footnotes:
⁵⁸ So GRA in agreement with Nazir VII, 1 (Sonc. ed., p. 177).
⁵⁹ Num. 6, 9ff.
⁶⁰ So H; V reads ‘an ordinary kohen
⁶¹ A man becomes a Nazirite by choice.
⁶² For the term of his vow.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:32

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:32](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:32)


הכל מודים בכ"ג ומרובה בבגדים יטמא מרובה בבגדים מרובה בבגדים והממונה יטמא הממונה [ממונה ומי שעבר יטמא מי שעבר] מי שעבר ומשוח מלחמה יטמא משוח מלחמה משוח מלחמה וכהן הדיוט מטמא כהן הדיוט כהן הדיוט ולוי מטמא לוי לוי וישראל מטמא ישראל ואם היו שניהן שוין מטמא הזריז שבהם ואם היו שניהם זריזין מטמא איזה מהן שירצה:


⁶³ All are agreed that [in the case of the High] Priest anointed with the oil of anointing⁶⁴ and [the High Priest robed] in many vestments,⁶⁵, p. 64. the latter must defile himself.⁶⁶ devolves upon him. As between a [High Priest robed] in many vestments and a deputy [High Priest] who had been anointed,⁶⁷ who acted as deputy of the High Priest when disqualified by uncleanness to officiate. When the High Priest returned to duty, the deputy did not revert to the rank of ordinary priest but retained the status of a High Priest with all the privileges of that office with two exceptions (cf. Meg. I, 9, Sonc. ed., p. 51). He was known as *kohen she‘abar*. See J. Rabbinowitz, *Mishnah Megillah*, p. 65. the latter must defile himself. As between a deputy [High Priest] and a *memunneh*,⁶⁸, the deputy High Priest; cf. Sanh. 19a (Sonc. ed., p. 97, n. 1). the latter must defile himself. As between a *memunneh* and a *kohen* anointed for war,⁶⁹ the latter must defile himself. As between a *kohen* anointed for war and an ordinary *kohen*, the latter must defile himself.⁷⁰ As between an ordinary *kohen* and a Levite, the Levite must defile himself.⁷¹ As between a Levite and a lay-Israelite, the latter must defile himself.⁷² If, however, the two [who find a *meth miẓwah*] are of equal status, the quicker of the two must defile himself; and if the two of them are [equally] quick, then whichever of them wishes to do so should defile himself.

footnotes:
⁶³ For the varying degrees of sanctity indicated in this Rule, cf. Nazir 47a (Sonc. ed., p. 177). The translation follows the emended text of GRA.
⁶⁴ All High Priests, beginning with Aaron, were consecrated with oil. During the reign of Josiah this ceremony ceased because the king had concealed the flask of special oil used for the ceremony (Hor. 12a, Sonc. ed., p. 85).
⁶⁵ During the Second Temple the High Priest was not anointed, and was only distinguished from the ordinary priests by wearing a larger number of vestments when officiating, eight as against their four. Hence he was designated ‘in many vestments’; cf. J. Rabbinowitz, Mishnah Megillah
⁶⁶ He being of lower sanctity, the duty of attending to a meth miẓwah
⁶⁷ A kohen
⁶⁸ Superintendent of the Temple Service, identified with the Segan
⁶⁹ A priest appointed to accompany the army into battle (Deut. 20, 2; cf. Soṭah VIII, 1, Sonc. ed., p. 207).
⁷⁰ Because the law against defilement exempts him in other contingencies (Lev. 21, 2f).
⁷¹ Because the Levite is under no restriction in this matter.
⁷² Since the Levite would be precluded from attending to his duties in the Temple for a period.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:33

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:33](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:33)


מצאוהו בין שדה ניר לשדה בור קוברו לשדה בור בין שדה ניר לשדה זרע קוברו לשדה ניר בין שדה זרע לשדה אילן קוברו לשדה זרע בין שדה אילן לשדה פרס קוברו בשדה אילן ואם היו שתיהן שוות קוברו בקרובה שבהן ואם לא היו שתיהן שוות קוברו באיזה מהן שירצה:


If they found [the body] between a ploughed field and a fallow field, they bury him in the fallow field;⁷³ between a ploughed field and a field sown with seeds, they bury him in the ploughed field; between a field sown with seeds and an orchard, they bury him in the field sown with seeds; between an orchard and a vineyard,⁷⁴ is the reading of GRA and H in agreement with j.Nazir VII, 1, 56b. they bury him in the orchard. If both are alike, they bury him in the nearer of the two; and if they are equidistant,⁷⁵ they bury him in whichever of them they desire.

footnotes:
⁷³ To interfere as little as possible with agriculture (B.Ḳ. 81b, Sonc. ed., pp. 463f).
⁷⁴ Kerem
⁷⁵ So H; V incorrectly ‘and if they are not alike’.


###### Tractate Semachot 4:34

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:34](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/4:34)


אמר ר׳ עקיבא זו היתה תחלת זכותי לפני חכמים השכמתי ומצאתי הרוג א׳ והייתי מטפל בו בג׳ תחומי שבת עד שהבאתיו למקום קבורה וקברתיו וכשבאתי והרצתי דברי לפני חכמים אמרו לי על כל פסיעה ופסיעה שהיית פוסע מעלין עליך כאילו שפכת דמים דנתי קל וחומר בעצמי ומה אם עכשיו שנתכוונתי לזכות חטאתי לרבות מעונותי אלו נתכוונתי על אחת כמה וכמה וכשהיה אדם מדבר זה לפני רבי עקיבא היה אומר זה היתה תחלת זכותי:


R. ‘Aḳiba said: This was the beginning of my ministration⁷⁶. before the Sages. I once went out early and found the body of a man who had been killed. I took charge of him for a distance of three Sabbath-limits⁷⁷ until I brought him to a place of burial and buried him. When I came and discussed the matter before the Sages, they said to me, ‘For each step you took it will be accounted to you as if you had committed murder’.⁷⁸ I then argued from the less to the greater as a result of my experience:⁷⁹, and the last word of the chapter is again changed to *tashmishi*. The text of V is in disorder. if when I intended to perform a meritorious act I rendered myself culpable, how much more so if I had not intended to perform a meritorious act!’ Whenever a person repeated this incident before R. ‘Aḳiba, he exclaimed, ‘This was the beginning of my ministration [before the Sages]’.

footnotes:
⁷⁶ H in conformity with j.Nazir VII, 1, 56a reads tashmishi
⁷⁷ A Sabbath-limit is two thousand cubits.
⁷⁸ He should have buried the corpse on the spot; B.Ḳ. 81a (Sonc. ed., p. 459).
⁷⁹ The next sentence is translated from the text of j.Nazir loc. cit.



## Chapter 5

###### Tractate Semachot 5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/5:1)


אבל כל שבעה ימים אסור לעשות מלאכה הוא ובניו ובנותיו ועבדיו ושפחותיו ובהמתו כשם שהם והוא אסור לעשות כך אחרים אסורים לעשות לו:


A mourner is forbidden to do work¹ throughout the seven days². 21b (Sonc. ed., p. 135) where the reading is ‘the first three days’. See, however, Tosafoth *ad loc., s.v. mikk’an*, where it is explained that this refers only to a very poor man. [of mourning], he, his sons and daughters,³ his male and female slaves and his cattle; and as he⁴ is forbidden to do work, so too others are forbidden to do work for him.

footnotes:
¹ Cf. M.Ḳ. 15b (Sonc. ed., p. 92) where by an interpretation of Amos 8, 10 and by the process of analogy this rule is arrived at.
² Cf. ibid
³ If they reside with him and are dependent upon him (Nachmanides).
⁴ The reading of GRA and H.


###### Tractate Semachot 5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/5:2)


עושין לו מלאכה שהיא אובדת (לו) כונסין לו גרנו ובועטין לו גתו גודפין לו את חביותיו זיתיו הפוכין רבי יהודה אומר טוען קורה הראשונה לאחר אבל גומר אמר לו רבי יוסי והלא אובדין הן אלא יגמור:


They may do work for him in connection with perishable things; they may gather his harvest for him, operate his wine-press for him and bung⁵ with H. his casks. If his olives had been turned,⁶ R. Judah says that he may put on the first⁷ beam and after his mourning he completes the work. R. Jose said to him, ‘But they are perishable things! He may complete [the whole operation in his mourning]’.

footnotes:
⁵ Reading gafin
⁶ For maturing before pressing or milling.
⁷ i.e. press them once.


###### Tractate Semachot 5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/5:3)


פשתנו לעלות מן המשרה צמרו לעלות מן היורה אם יש שם אחרים מסלקין עמו אם לאו מסלקין בצנעא:


With regard to his flax [which is ready] for lifting from the retting and his wool [ready] for lifting from the dye-bath, if there are others [to do the work] they should lift it,⁸ for V’s *‘immo*. but if not he may lift it in private.⁹

footnotes:
⁸ GRA reads ’otho
⁹ M.Ḳ. 11b (Sonc. ed., p. 63). ‘In private’ means behind closed doors.


###### Tractate Semachot 5:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/5:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/5:4)


בדו לגוף רבי יהודה אומר (למדין לאחר האבל ומריח) [עושין לו למודין ולאחר אבילות יגמור] אמר לו רבי יוסי והלא אובדים הם אלא יגמורו:


If his cask [of wine] is [ready] for bunging, R. Judah says that they cover it with shingles¹⁰ and after the mourning he completes [the work]. R. Jose said to him, ‘But it is perishable ! He¹¹ may complete [the whole operation in his mourning]’.

footnotes:
¹⁰ To prevent the wine from turning sour.
¹¹ So GRA for V’s ‘they’.


###### Tractate Semachot 5:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/5:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/5:5)


מרביצין שדה לבן שלא לזורעה. זורעין לו פס פשתן ברביעית כדברי ר"א וחכמים אומרים לא זרעה פשתן יזרענה מין אחר לא זרעה בשבת זה יזרענה בשבת אחרת:


They may besprinkle his white field¹² but may not¹³ sow it; they may sow for him a strip [of field] for a crop of flax when his turn for watering rights comes, according to R. Eliezer; but the Sages said: If he had not already sown it with flax, he should sow it with another kind [of crops], and if he does not sow it that week, he can sow it the following week.¹⁴

footnotes:
¹² i.e. a bright, shadeless vegetable or grain field, the opposite of an orchard (Jastrow).
¹³ H reads שלו for שלא, ‘his white field in order to sow it’. GRA emends: ‘but do not sow; and they may sow a field of flax for him in his turn for watering rights, according to R. Judah’, reading (so also H) רביעה for רביעית. Farmers took turns in drawing on the water-supply.
¹⁴ GRA reads: ‘if he cannot sow it on this watering turn, he can sow it on the next’. The Sages differ from R. Eliezer on both points, viz. they forbid the sprinkling of a white field and sowing flax during the mourning.


###### Tractate Semachot 5:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/5:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/5:6)


היה נחתום בעיר ואין שם אלא הוא עושה בצנעה מפני כבוד העם:


If he is a baker in the town and there is no other, he may do the work in private out of respect¹⁵ for the public.

footnotes:
¹⁵ GRA reads צורך, ‘needs’ instead of כבוד, ‘respect of’.


###### Tractate Semachot 5:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/5:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/5:7)


רבי שמעון בן יוחי אומר והרי שהיה ספר או בלן לרבים והגיע זמן הרגל עושה בצנעא מפני כבוד העם:


R. Simeon b. Yoḥai¹⁶ said: If he is a hair-dresser or a bath-attendant in the service of the public, and a Festival is close at hand, he may do the work in private out of respect for the public.

footnotes:
¹⁶ GRA reads ‘Gamaliel’ in agreement with M.Ḳ., loc. cit.


###### Tractate Semachot 5:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/5:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/5:8)


היה יושב ומספר ובאו ואמרו לו מת אביך הרי זה יגמור אחד המספר ואחד המסתפר. הפועלים שקבלו מלאכה לעשות לאבל בתוך ביתו אסור בתוך בתיהם מותר רבי שמעון בן יוחי אומר שכר יום בין כך ובין כך אסור [שכר הקיבולת בביתו אסור בבתיהם מותר במחובר לקרקע בין כך ובין כך אסור] בעיר אחרת בין כך ובין כך מותר:


If he is engaged in cutting a person’s hair and people come and tell him, ‘Your father is dead’, he should complete the work; this applies whether he is the hair-dresser or he is having his hair dressed. Workmen who have undertaken to do work for a mourner are forbidden to do it in his house but may do it in their own. R. Simeon b. Yoḥai¹⁷ said: A workman hired by the day is forbidden¹⁸ to do work in either [place]; a workman on a contract basis¹⁹ is forbidden [to do it in the mourner’s] house, but may do it in his own home. If it is work in connection with objects attached to the ground, it is forbidden [to do the work in either place];²⁰ in another town it is permitted in either case.²¹

footnotes:
¹⁷ GRA reads ‘Eleazar’.
¹⁸ Because he has the right to repudiate his bargain at any time.
¹⁹ i.e. a piece-worker.
²⁰ Because it is generally known that they are working for the mourner.
²¹ It is not generally so known.


###### Tractate Semachot 5:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/5:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/5:9)


אבל שתכפוהו אביליו הרי זה מיקל בסכין ובמספרת אבל לא במספרים ואם היו בגדיו צואין מדיחן בצונן אבל לא במי רגלים:
