###### Tractate Semachot 9:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:3)


על כל המתים כולן אינן חולצין אלא על אביו ועל אמו ואם אין ראויין לחלוץ אף על אביו ועל אמו לא יחלוץ מעשה כשמת אביו של ר"ע חלצו לפניו והוא לא חלץ.


⁸ One does not bare [the shoulder] for the dead in general but only for his father and mother; and if they are not worthy,⁹ Y.D., §340. he does not rend even for his father and mother. It is related that when R. ‘Aḳiba’s father died, those present bared [the shoulder], but he did not.

footnotes:
⁸ M.Ḳ. 22b (Sonc. ed., p. 140).
⁹ e.g. if the son is a distinguished scholar, as it would be undignified for him to be seen with bared shoulders; cf. Ṭur


###### Tractate Semachot 9:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:4)


על כל המתים כולן קורע בסכין על אביו ועל אמו אינו קורע בסכין.


For the dead in general one rends [the garments] with a knife,¹⁰ but for his father and mother he does not rend with a knife.¹¹

footnotes:
¹⁰ M.Ḳ. 22b (Sonc. ed., p. 143).
¹¹ But with the hand.


###### Tractate Semachot 9:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:5)


על כל המתים כולן אינו מגלה לבבו על אביו ועל אמו מגלה.


For the dead in general one does not bare his chest, but for his father and mother he bares it.¹² (Sonc. ed., p. 142). ‘Chest’ is lit. ‘heart’.

footnotes:
¹² M.Ḳ. loc. cit.


###### Tractate Semachot 9:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:6)


על כל המתים כולן. אינו מבדיל קמי שפה על אביו ועל אמו מבדיל קמי שפה א"ר יהודה כל קרע שאינו מבדיל קמי שפה הרי זה קרע של תיפלה (רבי יהודה אומר כל קרע שהוא בתחילה קורע שלשה אצבעות ותוספתו כל שהוא. אין קורעין אלא עד הטיבור הקטנים אחרים קורעים להם הקורע לשם כבוד הרי זה מאחה מיד. אפיקרסין אין מעכבין את הקריעה רבי בנימין משום ר"ע אומר קורעין אפיקרסין).


For the dead in general one does not divide¹³. the [upper] selvage-border [of his garment],¹⁴ but for his father and mother he does divide it. R. Judah said: Any rending [of a garment] which does not divide the selvage-border is mere make-believe. R. Judah said:¹⁵ Any initial rending must be [to the extent of] three fingers, while the extension may be as small as he cares.¹⁶ One only rends as far down as the region of the navel.¹⁷ In the case of children, others rend for them.¹⁸ One who rends [not as a relative but] from respect [for the dead] may stitch [the edges] forthwith. The rending of one’s undershirt is not indispensable.¹⁹ R. Benjamin said in the name of R. ‘Aḳiba: One must rend the undershirt.

footnotes:
¹³ ibid
¹⁴ i.e. about the neck and shoulders.
¹⁵ M.Ḳ. 26b (Sonc. ed., p. 169).
¹⁶ lit. ‘whatever it be’, i.e. when the tear is extended for another death.
¹⁷ If a succession of deaths occurs, that is the limit to which the tear may extend.
¹⁸ The reading in M.Ḳ. is ‘children are made to rend’; not that they are under the obligation to do so, but in order to arouse sadness in those who witness it.
¹⁹ Cf. M.Ḳ. 22b (Sonc. ed., p. 142).


###### Tractate Semachot 9:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:7)


על כל המתים כולם הוא קורע את העליון על אביו ועל אמו אע"פ שיש עליו מאה קורעין. האשה אינה קורעת אלא את העליון רבי יהודה אומר האשה קורעת את הפנימי והופכתו לאחוריה וחוזרת וקורעת את השאר


²⁰. For the dead in general one rends only the uppermost [garment], but for his father and mother he must rend [all] even though he be wearing a hundred. A woman only rends her uppermost [garment]. R. Judah said: A woman rends her undergarment, turns it front to back, and then rends the remaining [garments].²¹

footnotes:
²⁰ ibid
²¹ So as not to expose her chest.


###### Tractate Semachot 9:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:8)


[על כל המתים שולל לאחר ז׳ ומאחה אחר ל' ועל אב ואם אינו מאחה לעולם. האשה שוללת לאחר ז׳ ומאחה אחר ל׳ ר"י אומר האשה שוללת מיד ומאחה אחר ג']


For the dead generally one tacks²² the rent together after seven days, and [completely] restitches [the edges] after thirty; but for one’s father and mother one never restitches [the edges]. A woman tacks the rent together after seven [days] and restitches [the edges] after thirty. R. Judah said: A woman tacks the rent together immediately²³). and restitches [the edges] after seven [days].²⁴

footnotes:
²² With large basting stitches.
²³ From a sense of self-respect (M.Ḳ. loc. cit.
²⁴ So GRA and H; V reads ‘three’.


###### Tractate Semachot 9:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:9)


על כל המתים כולן הוא דוחק את המטה ואינו מרבה בעסקו על אביו ועל אמו המרבה בעסקו ואינו דוחק הרי זה משובח אבל אם היתה שעת דוחק או ליל שבת או גשמים יורדין דוחק אף על אביו ועל אמו את המטה ואינו מרבה בעסקו.


²⁵ For the dead generally one expedites²⁶ the bier and does not occupy himself unduly with their affairs;²⁷ but in the case of his father and mother, whoever greatly occupies himself with their affairs and does not expedite the bier is praiseworthy. But if time is pressing, or the Sabbath-eve is approaching, or rain falls, he may expedite the bier for his father and mother and does not unduly occupy himself with their affairs.

footnotes:
²⁵ Cf. M.Ḳ. 22a (Sonc. ed., p. 140).
²⁶ i.e. hastens the interment.
²⁷ viz. their business affairs before the funeral (Rashi). R. Ḥananel, on the basis of a passage in j.M.Ḳ. III, 8, 83d, explains ‘reduces the funeral expenses and the lament to a minimum’.


###### Tractate Semachot 9:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:10)


על כל המתים כולן אסור ללבוש כלים מגוהצים עד שלשים יום על אביו ואמו אסור עד שיגיע הרגל ויגערו בו חביריו.


For the dead in general it is forbidden to wear pressed clothes until thirty days [are completed], but in the case of his father and mother it is forbidden until the [next] Festival occurs and then only if his companions rebuke him.²⁸

footnotes:
²⁸ On his untidy appearance.


###### Tractate Semachot 9:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:11)


על כל המתים כולן אסור בתספורת עד שישלמו שלשים יום על אביו ועל אמו ישלח פרע עד שיגערו בו חביריו.


²⁹ For the dead generally it is forbidden to trim one’s hair until thirty days are completed; but in the case of his father and mother, one lets the hair grow long until his companions rebuke him.

footnotes:
²⁹ M.Ḳ. 22b (Sonc. ed., p. 141).


###### Tractate Semachot 9:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:12)


על כל המתים כולן אסור בסחורה עד שישלמו לו שלשים יום על אביו ועל אמו עד שיגערו בו חביריו ואמרו לו צא עמנו.


For the dead generally one is forbidden to engage in trade³⁰ until thirty days are completed; but for one’s father and mother until his companions rebuke him and say to him, ‘Come out with us’.

footnotes:
³⁰ Not ordinary trade which is permissible after seven days of mourning, but to set out on a business journey to distant lands, because that might be interpreted as an indication of callousness.


###### Tractate Semachot 9:13

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:13)


על כל המתים כולן אסור לילך לבית המשתה עד שישלמו לו ל׳ יום על אביו יעל אמו אסור כל שנים עשר חדש אלא א"כ היתה לשם שמים:


³¹ For the dead generally it is forbidden to go to a house of feasting until thirty days are completed; but for one’s father and mother, all the twelve months unless it is for a sacred cause.³²

footnotes:
³¹ Cf. M.Ḳ. loc. cit.
³² e.g. a wedding.


###### Tractate Semachot 9:14

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:14](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:14)


אמרו לו מת אביו וקרע והיא אינו אלא אמו יצא אמו וקרע והוא אינו אלא אביו (לא) יצא מת אביו וקרע והוא אינו אלא אחד מן הקרובים יצא אחד מן הקרובים והוא אינו אלא אביו לא יצא


If news reached him that his father died and he rent, but [in fact] it was his mother, he has done his duty.³³ [If news reached him that] his mother died, but it was his father and he rent, he has done his duty. [If news reached him that] his father died and he rent, but it was one of his near relatives, he has done his duty. [If news reached him that] one of his near relatives died, but it was his father, he has not done his duty.³⁴

footnotes:
³³ He has not to rend again.
³⁴ He has to rend again.


###### Tractate Semachot 9:15

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:15](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:15)


מי שמתו אביו ואמו קורע קרע אחד לשניהם רבי יהודה בן תימא אומר קורע על זה בפני עצמו ועל זו בפני עצמה מי שמתו עשרה בניו כאחת קורע קרע אחד על כולן משקרע בישרו אותו שמת בנו וקרע הרי זה קורע והולך עד שהוא מגיע לטיבור הגיע לטיבור הופך את הקרע לאחוריו מת בנו וקרע הרי זה מבדיל בין הקרע ג' אצבעות וקורע


If one’s father and mother died [at the same time], he makes only one rent for the two of them.³⁵ R. Judah b. Tema said: He rends for each separately. If one’s ten children died at the same time, he makes one rent for all of them.
³⁶.If after he had made a rent [for another] news reached him that his son died, he continues to rend until it [reaches down] to the navel; when it has reached the navel he turns [the garment] front to back.
If his son died and he rent [and news of another death reached him], he leaves a space of three fingers and again rends.

footnotes:
³⁵ Cf. M.Ḳ. 26b (Sonc. ed., p. 170).
³⁶ ibid


###### Tractate Semachot 9:16

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:16](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:16)


אין קורעין אלא בין שתי הקורע למטה מן הצדדין לא יצא רבי יהודה אומר יצא אמר רבי יהודה מעשה בימי חרסום שהיו קורעין למטה מן הצדדין אמרו לו אין שעת הסכנה ראיה. פוחח אינו קורע רבי יהודה אומר קורע את הברסיס ואת הבדרסים ואת הפלמוט ואת השלל ואת החרוד לא יצא הקורע את המאחוי יצא איזהו מאחוי כל שיאחהו כל צרכו.


³⁷ The rent must be made only between the two borders;³⁸ one who makes the rent along the border or at the side has not done his duty.³⁹ R. Judah said: He has done his duty. R. Judah said: It happened in the days of Ḥarsum⁴⁰ which means ‘prohibition to travel’, e.g. in a time of war. that people used to make the rent at the lower end [of the garment] and at the side. His colleagues retorted, ‘No proof may be adduced [from a practice adopted] in a time of danger’.
A man dressed in torn garments does not rend.⁴¹ R. Judah said: One who rends a cloak of *Birrus*,⁴² a Brundisian cloak,⁴³ a *Poenula*,⁴⁴ a stitched garment or a veil,⁴⁵ has not done his duty.⁴⁶ One who rends a basted garment⁴⁷ has done his duty. What constitutes a basted garment? [When its tear] has been completely basted.

footnotes:
³⁷ The text is corrupt, and the translation follows the version of Nachmanides (cf. N.Y.).
³⁸ On either side of the front opening of the shirt or tunic.
³⁹ Because the rent is hidden.
⁴⁰ The name of a High Priest who lived in a time of persecution. GRA reads ḥerum
⁴¹ Since his garments are already torn, the new rent will not be distinguishable.
⁴² ‘A cloth named after a Celtic people, Burri’ (Krauss); ‘a cloak of thick woollen material’ (Jastrow).
⁴³ A cloak thicker than the previous material (Jastrow); a cloth named after a Celtic people, the Burridens (Krauss).
⁴⁴ A travelling cloak (Jastrow).
⁴⁵ Jastrow emends חרוד to רדיד, a garment of fine texture.
⁴⁶ These materials are either too thick or too fine for the rent to be distinguishable.
⁴⁷ i.e. one sewn with large stitches.


###### Tractate Semachot 9:17

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:17](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:17)


אלו קרעין שאין מתאחין על אביו ועל אמו ועל רבו שלמדו חכמה ועל שריפת התורה ועל חורבן הבית ועל שמועות רעות ועל קללת השם והרואה את ירושלים מן הצופים ועל המלך ועל הנשיא ויש אומרים אף על כהן גדול ונביא ועל אב בית דין על שריפת התורה לא ששמע שנשרפה אלא א"כ עומד עליה והיא נשרפת על חרבן הבית אפילו שמע שחרב הבית ועל שמועות הרעות שהן קשין כחרבן הבית. הרואה את ירושלים מן הצופים חייב לקרוע נכנס בתוכה הרי זה מוסיף על הקרע עלה מוסיף לקרוע עלה להודיעו על פרק ופרק חייב לקרוע עלה וישב בה קורע קרע אחד ויוצא.


These are the rents which may not be basted: [When it was done] for his father or mother, for his teacher who instructed him in wisdom, for a scroll of the Torah which has been burnt, for the ruined Temple, for evil tidings, [on hearing] the Divine Name blasphemed, on seeing Jerusalem from Mount Scopus,⁴⁸ and [on the death of] a *Nasi*’. Some add: [On the death of] a High Priest, a prophet, or an *’Ab Beth Din*. As regards the burning of a scroll of the Torah, [the garment is rent] not on hearing of this happening but only when he stands by while it burns. As for the ruined Temple, [he rends his garment] even if he heard that it has been destroyed; similarly with evil tidings because they are as grievous as the destruction of the Temple.
One who sees Jerusalem from Mount Scopus must rend; when he enters [the city] he extends the rent, and if he goes up [again to Jerusalem] he must extend it further. ‘If he goes up again’—this is to teach that he is obliged to make a rent every time he goes up [to Jerusalem] during each period [of thirty days].⁴⁹ If he came up [to Jerusalem] and settled there, he rends once and has thereby done his duty.

footnotes:
⁴⁸ Because from there the ruins of Jerusalem are visible.
⁴⁹ [The meaning is illustrated by j.Ber. IX, 2, 13d: R. Simeon Ḳamṭraya asked R. Ḥiyya b. Abba, ‘Since I am an ass-driver and go up to Jerusalem throughout the year, how is it about rending my garment?’ He replied, ‘Within thirty days [after a visit] it is unnecessary for you to rend’.]


###### Tractate Semachot 9:18

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:18](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:18)


הלוקח חלוק קרוע לחבירו [אינו רשאי לאחות אלא אם כן הוא יודע שהוא מן הקרעים המתאחין המוכר חלוק לחבירו] צריך להודיעו שהוא מן הקרעין [המתאחין או] שאין מתאחין (וכן) כל הקרעין שאין מתאחין אסור למכרן לנכרים לתופרן ולהפכן מלמטה דברי ר"ש ורבי אליעזר מתיר ר"ג אומר השואל חלוק מחבירו לילך ולבקר בו את אביו הלך ומצאו שמת הרי זה קורע ומאחהו ומחזיר לו את חלוקו. לקחו הימנו סתם אינו יוצא לקרוע


One who buys a tunic which has been rent [by a mourner] from⁵⁰ his companion may not baste it unless he knows that the rent is of the kind which may be basted. One who sells a tunic which has been rent [by a mourner] to his companion must inform him whether the rent is of the kind which may be basted or not.⁵¹ All rent [garments] which may not be basted must not be sold to non-Jews⁵² or stitched together or be reversed from top to bottom, according to the view of R. Simeon; but R. Eliezer permits it to be done. ⁵³ (Sonc. ed., p. 172).One who borrows a tunic from his companion to go and visit his father and when he arrives finds that his father died, he rends it, bastes it and returns it to his companion. If, however, he borrowed it without specifying for what purpose he needed it, he is not permitted⁵⁴ to rend it.

footnotes:
⁵⁰ So GRA and H; V has ‘for’.
⁵¹ Cf. M.Ḳ. 26b (Sonc. ed., p. 169).
⁵² Lest they resell it to a Jew.
⁵³ Cf. M.Ḳ. loc. cit.
⁵⁴ This is the reading of GRA and H.


###### Tractate Semachot 9:19

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:19](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:19)


היוצא בחלוק קרוע לפני מטתו של מת הרי זה גוזל את המת מרובה גזילת המת מגזילת החי שהגוזל את החי הוא יכול לפייסו ולהחזיר לו את הגזלות [הגוזל את המת אינו יכול לפייסו ולהחזיר לו גזילו] הקורע על מתו וחזרה בו נשמה אם לאלתר מת יצא ואם לאחר זמן מת לא יצא [כיוצא בדבר אתה אומר מכבד אביו ואמו במותן כאילו כבדן בחייהן משום שהמכבדן בחייהן משום יראה ומשום ירושה והמכבדן במותן אין מכבדן אלא לשם שמים]


⁵⁵ One who goes out before the bier of the dead with a garment already rent robs the dead [of his due]. More grievous is the robbing of the dead than robbing the living, because one who robs the living can appease him by making restitution, but one who robs the dead cannot appease him by making restitution.
⁵⁶Likewise you may say: One who honours his father and mother after their death⁵⁷ is as if he honoured them in their lifetime, since one who honours them while they are alive may do so from fear or for the sake of an inheritance; but he who honours them after their death does so in the name of Heaven only.⁵⁸
He who made a rent for one [presumed] dead and he revived, if he dies immediately afterwards [the man who made a rent] has fulfilled his duty, but if [death occurred] after some time he has not done his duty.⁵⁹

footnotes:
⁵⁵ Cf. M.Ḳ. loc. cit.
⁵⁶ This sentence and the next Rule are transposed in accordance with GRA. A better sequence is thereby obtained.
⁵⁷ Cf. Ḳid. 31b (Sonc. ed., p. 154).
⁵⁸ To fulfil God’s command and without a motive of self-interest.
⁵⁹ Cf. Ned. 87a (Sonc. ed., p. 268).


###### Tractate Semachot 9:20

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:20](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/9:20)


כל המציל כלים מן המת הרי זה גוזל את המת יש מציל ויש שאינו מציל אם עד שלא הגיע לארון מציל ואם משהגיע לארון אינו מציל אבל מלמדין לאדם שלא יהא חובלן והלא אמרו כל המרבה כלים על המת הרי זה עובר משום בל תשחית דברי ר"מ ר"א בר צדוק אומר מנוולו רבן גמליאל אומר מרבה עליו רימה רבי נתן אומר היא כסותו שיורדת עמו לשאול היא באה עמו לעתיד לבוא שנאמר (איוב ל״ח:י״ד) תתהפך כחומר חותם ויתיצבו כמו לבוש:


Whosoever rescues garments from the dead⁶⁰ robs the dead. There are times when a man may rescue them and times when he may not. [48*b*] If the garments have not touched the bier he may rescue them, but if they have touched the bier he may not. We, however, instruct people not to seize them.⁶¹ And whoever piles up garments on the dead commits a transgression because of the law which forbids wilful destruction,⁶² according to the view of R. Meir. R. Eliezer b. Ẓadoḳ said: It disgraces him. Rabban Gamaliel said: He causes maggots to breed upon [the dead]. R. Nathan said: The garment which goes down with him to the grave will come back with him in the time to come,⁶³ as it is stated, *It is changed as clay under the seal; and they stand as a garment*.⁶⁴, is homiletically read as לָבו·שׁ, ‘as he is dressed’.

footnotes:
⁶⁰ Cf. Sanh. 48a, b (Sonc. ed., pp. 321f). If parents piled on the bier garments belonging to their child as an expression of grief and as a sign that they renounced everything that belonged to him. Saving the garments constituted an act of returning lost property; but when the articles touched the bier they were regarded as designated for the dead.
⁶¹ V inserts ‘but did not [the Sages] say’ which GRA deletes.
⁶² The Rabbis derived a law against the wanton destruction of articles from Deut. 20, 19; cf. Shab. 129a (Sonc. ed., p. 644, n. 1).
⁶³ In the Resurrection; cf. j.Keth. XII, 3, 35a, Sanh. 90b (Sonc. ed., p. 607).
⁶⁴ Job 38, 14; לְבו·שׁ, a garment



## Chapter 10

###### Tractate Semachot 10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/10:1)


אבל כל זמן שמתו מוטל לפניו פטור מקריאת שמע ומן התפילה ומכל מצות האמורות בתורה ואם רצה להחמיר על עצמו הרי זה לא יחמיר מפני כבוד המת


A mourner, as long as his dead is lying before him,¹ is exempt from reading the *Shema‘* and *Tefillah* and from all the commandments prescribed in the Torah. Even if he desires to place himself under greater restrictions [than the law requires] he should not do so out of respect for the dead.

footnotes:
¹ i.e. before the burial for which he has to make arrangements; cf. Ber. 18a (Sonc. ed., p. 106).


###### Tractate Semachot 10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/10:2)


הגיע זמן קריאת שמע כל העם קורין והוא שותק עמדו להתפלל הכל מתפללים והוא מצדיק עליו את הדין ומה הוא אומר רבון כל העולמים חטאתי לפניך כמעט מחובותי נגביתי והלא הרבה מכן הייתי שוה יהי רצון מלפניך שתגדור פרצותינו ותנחמנו רבי שמעון בן אלעזר אומר אף הוא שואל לכל צורכי המת.


² When the time comes for reading the *Shema‘*, all the people [present] read it but he remains silent; when they stand to read the *Tefillah*, all read it but he justifies for himself the divine decree. What does he say? ‘Sovereign of all the universe, I have sinned before Thee; I have repaid but few of my obligations; I merit much more punishment than this; may it be Thy will to repair our breaches and to comfort us’. R. Simeon b. Eleazar said: He also prays for all the needs of the dead person.

footnotes:
² Cf. Ber. 19a (Sonc. ed., p. 116).


###### Tractate Semachot 10:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/10:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/10:3)


אבל אוכל אצל חבירו ואם אין לו חבר אוכל בבית אחר ואם אין לו בית אחר עושה לו מחיצה ואוכל ואם אין יכול לעשות לו מחיצה הופך פניו מן המת ואוכל ואינו מיסב ואוכל ואינו אוכל כל צרכו ואינו אוכל בשר ואינו שותה יין ואין מזמנים עליו ואין אומרים לו ברך בד"א בחול אבל בשבת אוכל כל צרכו ואוכל בשר ושותה יין ומזמנים עליו ר"ג אומר אבל בשבת כאילו שאינו אבל.


³. 17b-18a (Sonc. ed., p. 102), M.Ḳ. 23b (Sonc. ed., p. 146). A mourner eats [his meal] in the house of his neighbour, and if he has no neighbour he eats it in another room; and if he has no other room [than that in which the dead is lying], he puts up a partition and eats; and if he is not able to put up a partition, he turns his face away from the dead and eats. But he must not eat in a reclining posture,⁴ eat as much food as he needs, eat meat or drink wine. He is not included in the *Zimmun*, others do not say Grace on his behalf, and we do not request him, ‘Say Grace’.⁵ When does this apply? On week-days, but on the Sabbath⁶ he eats as much as he needs, he may eat meat and drink wine, and is included in the *Zimmun*. Rabban Gamaliel said: A mourner [is regarded] on the Sabbath as if he were not a mourner.

footnotes:
³ ibid
⁴ At meals people usually reclined on the left elbow on a couch as a mark of ease.
⁵ This means to lead the company in Grace after meals; but the Gemara, Ber. 17b (Sonc. ed., p. 107), reads ‘and he does not say a blessing [over food]’, which Rashi interprets as referring to Grace before meals.
⁶ When there is no outward mourning.


###### Tractate Semachot 10:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/10:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/10:4)


אין מוציאין את המת סמוך לקריאת שמע אלא א"כ הקדימו שעה אחת (או שאחרו שעה אחת)


We do not remove the body [for burial] near [the time of] reading the *Shema‘*, unless it is an hour before.⁷

footnotes:
⁷ V and H add ‘or they postpone it for an hour’ which GRA deletes.


###### Tractate Semachot 10:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/10:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/10:5)


הוציאו סבלי המטה את שלפני המטה את שלאחר המטה את שלפני המטה צורך בהן פטורין מן ק"ש ומותרין בנעילת הסנדל אלו ואלו פטורין מן התפלה (ומן התפילין)


⁸ Once the bearers of the bier have carried [the body] out—such as are before the bier⁹ and such as are behind it¹⁰—those who are before the bier, whose services are still needed, are exempt from reading the *Shema‘* and are forbidden¹¹ and, permitted to wear shoes’. to wear shoes. All alike are exempt from the *Tefillah* and from putting on the *tefillin*.

footnotes:
⁸ Cf. Ber. 17b (Sonc. ed., p. 106).
⁹ Awaiting their turn to relieve the bearers.
¹⁰ These have already carried the coffin and so have done their duty.

קברו את המת וחזרו אם יכולין להתחיל ולגמור עד שלא יגיעו לשורה עומדין ואחר כך קורין ואם לאו קורין ומתפללין


¹² Having buried the dead and returned [from the graveside], if they are able to begin and complete [the reading of the *Shema‘*] before arriving at the row,¹³ they read¹⁴ it and afterwards stand in the row; but if not, they stand in the row and afterwards read [the *Shema‘*] and the *Tefillah*.

ואחר כך עומדים בשורה. שורה הפנימית הרואה פני אבלים פטורה והחיצונה חייבת ר"י אומר אפי׳ אין שם אלא שורה אחת העומדים לשם אבל פטורים לשם כבוד ירדו לפסגן הרואים פני [האבל] פטורים ושאינם רואים פני האבל חייבין


¹⁵ The inner row [of the comforters] who can see the mourners is exempt [from reading the *Shema‘*], but the outer row is under the obligation. R. Judah said: Even if there be [only] one row, those who stand for the sake of [comforting] the mourner are exempt, but those who stand for the sake of [their own] honour¹⁶ reads ‘for their own purposes’, i.e. to see the crowd. [are under the obligation].¹⁷ If they came to the memorial address,¹⁸ those who can see the mourners are exempt and those who cannot see them are under the obligation.

¹⁶ Ber. loc. cit.

###### Tractate Semachot 10:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/10:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/10:8)


ר"ש בן אלעזר אומר קבר שאולה היתה לר"ג ביבנה שהיו מכניסים את המת לתוכה ונועלין את הדלת לפניו ובאין ועומדים בשורה (ומזמנים) [ומנחמין] ופוטרין את הרבים ואח"כ מעלין אותן לירושלם


R. Simeon b. Eleazar said: Rabban Gamaliel had a borrowed grave¹⁹ in Jabneh where they brought the dead and locked the door behind it. Men came, stood in the row and comforted the mourners and the public was thereby exempt [from this duty]. Later [the bodies] were conveyed to Jerusalem [for burial].

###### Tractate Semachot 10:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/10:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/10:9)


נסתם הגולל מכסה את הראש [בא לעמוד בשורה מגלה את ראשו ופוטרן יצא חוץ לשורה מכסה] את ראשו נכנס לתוך ביתו באו אחרים לנחמו מגלה את ראשו ופוטרן.


After [the tomb] was closed with the top stone, [the mourner] muffles his head.²⁰ Should he take his stand in the row,²¹ he unmuffles his head and exempts others [from standing in the row for him]. When he leaves the row he muffles his head. After he has entered his house, should others come to comfort him, he unmuffles his head and exempts them.

חכם או תלמיד חכם שמת לו מת באין ויושבין לפניו ונושאין ונותנין בהלכות אבלים ואם טעו משיבן בשפה רפה והוא אינו שואל אם רצה מדבר ע"י עצמו ואם לאו נותן לאחר רשות ומדבר על ידו. מעשה שמת בנו של רבי יהודה ונתן רשות לאחר שידבר על ידו מעשה (בבנו) [שמת בנו] של ר׳ יוסי הגלילי ונתן רשות לאחר שידבר על ידו מעשה שמת בנו של ר"ע והיה יושב ודורש כל היום זה מעשה היה ביד רבי והיה דורש כל היום.

Should they make a mistake he corrects them in a low tone, but he does not ask questions.²³ If he so desire he may speak personally to them, and if not he appoints someone to be his spokesman. It is related that when his son died, R. Judah appointed someone to be his spokesman. It is related that when his son died, R. Jose the Galilean appointed someone to be his spokesman. It is related that when his son died, R. ‘Aḳiba continued his discourse all the day.²⁴ The same is also reported of Rabbi [Judah the Prince]; he continued his discourse all the day.

אבל שבת הראשונה אינו נכנס לבית הכנסת שניה נכנס ואינו יושב במקומו שלישית נכנס ויושב במקומו ואינו מדבר רביעית שוה לכל אדם רבי שמעון אומר בראשונה (ובשניה) אחרים באין אצלו ומנחמים אותו שניה היא כראשונה שלישית היא כשניה רביעית שוה לכל אדם (ר"מ אומר בראשונה ובשניה אינו בא לבית הכנסת שלישית נכנס ויושב במקומו רביעית שוה לכל אדם) במה דברים אמורים בבית הכנסת אבל בבית המדרש עולה ואפילו בשבת ראשונה


²⁵ In the first week a mourner does not enter the Synagogue; in the second week he enters but does not sit in his [usual] place; in the third week he enters and sits in his [usual] place but does not speak; in the fourth week he is like any other person. R. Simeon said: In the first week²⁶ people come up to him to comfort him; the second week is treated like the first, the third like the second, and in the fourth week he is like any other person. R. Meir said: In the first and second week he does not enter the Synagogue, in the third he enters and sits in his [usual] place, and in the fourth he is like any other person. To what does this apply? To a Synagogue, but as for a House of Study he may enter it even in the first week.

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/10:13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tractate%20Semachot/r/10:13)

²⁸ When a Sage dies, his *Beth Hammidrash* goes into vacation and all the others change their place. When a *Nasi’* dies, all the Houses of Study go into vacation; people do not walk about in the street but enter [their homes] and sit in silence overcome by distress like men without a leader. When the time of rising arrives, they stand up and reduce their affairs to a minimum. The people of the Synagogue enter the Synagogue [on the Sabbath] and seven persons read the weekly portion of the Torah and go away.
